Retrying LLM API calls: exponential backoff, jitter, Retry-After and model fallbacks

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 18 • Originally published at aliahmed.si

LLM APIs fail in predictable ways under load: 429 when you exceed a rate limit, 5xx when the provider has a problem, an "overloaded" response when capacity is short (Anthropic uses 529 for that), and timeouts. A good client retries only those, waits with exponential backoff and full jitter so thousands of clients don't retry in lockstep, honours Retry-After when the provider sends it, gives up when a retry budget runs out, and only then falls back to another model. It never retries a 400: a malformed request fails the same way every time and on every model. Below is a small, tested implementation.

A 400 from an over-long prompt is a context-window problem, which is solved by trimming the conversation, not by waiting.

Plain exponential backoff (wait 0.5 s, 1 s, 2 s, 4 s) has a flaw: when an outage hits every client at the same moment, they all retry at the same moments too, and the provider gets the traffic in synchronised waves. Full jitter picks a random wait between zero and the exponential cap, which spreads retries out. In AWS's published comparison of backoff strategies, full jitter was one of the two that performed best, and the simplest.

```js title="retry.mjs" const RETRYABLE = new Set([408, 429, 500, 502, 503, 504, 529]);

export class HttpError extends Error { constructor(status, retryAfter) { super(HTTP ${status}); this.status = status; this.retryAfter = retryAfter; // seconds, from the Retry-After header } }

const isRetryable = (error) => error instanceof HttpError ? RETRYABLE.has(error.status) : error.name === "TimeoutError" || error.code === "ECONNRESET";

/** Full-jitter exponential backoff that honours Retry-After, with a cap on total time spent waiting. / export async function withRetry(call, { attempts = 4, baseMs = 500, maxMs = 20_000, budgetMs = 30_000, sleep, random = Math.random } = {}) { let waited = 0; for (let attempt = 1; ; attempt++) { try { return await call(attempt); } catch (error) { if (attempt >= attempts || !isRetryable(error)) throw error; const backoff = random() * Math.min(maxMs, baseMs * 2 * (attempt - 1)); const delay = error.retryAfter ? Math.max(error.retryAfter * 1000, backoff) : backoff; if (waited + delay > budgetMs) throw error; waited += delay; await sleep(delay); } } }

Fallbacks change behaviour, so treat them deliberately: the fallback model should be tested with your prompts, handle your tool schemas, and fit the same context window. Log which model answered each request, and include it in cost reporting. Comparing by model name alone can hide which deployment did the work: see why LLM cost dashboards lie when deployments share a model name.

```js title="retry.test.mjs" import assert from "node:assert/strict";

const sleeps = []; const opts = { sleep: async (ms) => void sleeps.push(ms), random: () => 1 };

// 503, 503, then success: waits of 500 and 1000 ms. let n = 0; assert.equal(await withRetry(async () => (++n < 3 ? Promise.reject(new HttpError(503)) : "ok"), opts), "ok"); assert.deepEqual(sleeps.splice(0), [500, 1000]);

// 429 with Retry-After: 7 waits 7 s, not 0.5 s. n = 0; await withRetry(async () => (++n < 2 ? Promise.reject(new HttpError(429, 7)) : "ok"), opts); assert.deepEqual(sleeps.splice(0), [7000]);

// 400 is never retried. n = 0; await assert.rejects( withRetry(async () => { n++; throw new HttpError(400); }, opts), /HTTP 400/ ); assert.equal(n, 1);

// Retry-After beyond the budget: give up now instead of sleeping. await assert.rejects( withRetry(async () => { throw new HttpError(429, 120); }, opts), /HTTP 429/ );

Ultraprocessed Foods Alter Food-Reward Brain Activity

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A controlled feeding trial reveals that just two weeks on a diet high in ultraprocessed foods alters brain reward responses in emerging adults ages 18 to 21, while leaving adults ages 22 to 25 unaffected. The fMRI study shows blunted activation in the developing orbitofrontal cortex, a key region for food valuation and self-control, even when diets are strictly matched for calories, sugar, fat, and nutrients.

Ultraprocessed foods, products formulated with industrial processing methods and ingredients rarely found in a home kitchen, account for more than half of the calories consumed by young adults in the United States. While extensive research links these foods to obesity, metabolic syndrome, and cardiovascular disease, neuroscientists are increasingly uncovering how industrial processing directly interacts with the developing human brain.

In a controlled feeding study published this month in the journal Obesity, researchers at Virginia Tech’s Fralin Biomedical Research Institute at VTC and Department of Human Nutrition, Foods, and Exercise found that emerging adults ages 18 to 21 exhibit distinct neural changes in food-reward circuitry after consuming an ultraprocessed diet for just two weeks.

Led by first author Dr. Emma Leslie alongside neuroscientist Dr. Alex DiFeliceantonio and registered dietitian Dr. Brenda Davy, the findings complement earlier results from the same trial showing that 18- to 21-year-olds consumed excess calories at a buffet meal after eating ultraprocessed foods, even when they were no longer hungry.

To separate the effects of industrial food processing from basic nutritional content, the researchers recruited 22 healthy adults ages 18 to 25 (including seven participants ages 21 or younger) for a randomized crossover feeding trial. Each participant followed two strictly controlled diets for two weeks apiece, separated by a four-week washout period:

Critically, both diets were engineered to have identical macronutrient profiles (50% carbohydrates, 35% fat, 15% protein), matching levels of fiber, added sugars, saturated fat, sodium, energy density, and overall nutritional quality, calibrated to keep each participant’s body weight stable.

Before and after each two-week dietary phase, participants underwent functional MRI (fMRI) brain scans while receiving small sips of an ultraprocessed milkshake or a tasteless control solution through a specialized delivery tube.

When the researchers analyzed the neuroimaging data, they discovered a striking developmental divide:

“Among the younger participants, consuming a diet high in ultraprocessed foods for just two weeks was associated with decreased brain response to a rewarding food — a milkshake — in the orbitofrontal cortex,” explained Leslie, who conducted the work as a doctoral student at Virginia Tech and is now a postdoctoral associate at the University of Michigan.

Located just behind the eyes, the orbitofrontal cortex is one of the final regions of the human brain to finish maturing, continuing to remodel into the mid- to late 20s. It plays a central role in evaluating food rewards, regulating cravings, and exerting executive self-control to stop eating.

Because the two diets were matched across nutritional metrics, the findings point toward non-nutritive properties of ultraprocessed foods, such as chemical additives, emulsifiers, texture alterations, or compounds generated during industrial processing, as potential drivers of altered brain signaling.

“This study is important because you have this change in an area of the brain known to govern self-control and potentially put a stop to eating,” said DiFeliceantonio, associate professor at the Fralin Biomedical Research Institute and interim co-director of its Center for Health Behaviors Research. “This is giving us insight into how ultraprocessed foods are affecting the bodies and brains of younger people, who might be especially vulnerable to their effects. And that’s important to know for things like dietary guidelines, doctor recommendations, and policy decisions.”

While the researchers note that the sample size was small and the age-based findings were exploratory, and did not directly correlate individual fMRI shifts with buffet calorie intake, the results highlight adolescence and early adulthood as critical windows of neurodevelopmental sensitivity.

Next, DiFeliceantonio and Davy plan to investigate whether adolescents under 18, who consume the highest proportion of ultraprocessed foods of any U.S. demographic, show even stronger neural sensitivity, and whether brain responses recover once individuals return to whole-food diets.

How do ultraprocessed foods affect the brains of young adults?

A Virginia Tech study found that after eating a diet high in ultraprocessed foods for just two weeks, young adults ages 18 to 21 showed decreased brain activity in the orbitofrontal cortex, a region involved in food reward, valuation, and self-control, when tasting a milkshake. Adults ages 22 to 25 did not show these brain changes.

Was the brain effect simply caused by extra sugar, fat, or calories?

No. Both the ultraprocessed diet and the unprocessed comparison diet contained the exact same amounts of calories, carbohydrates, fat, protein, added sugars, saturated fat, fiber, and sodium. This suggests that industrial food processing or additives themselves, rather than basic nutrients, drove the brain changes.

Why are 18- to 21-year-olds more sensitive to ultraprocessed foods than slightly older adults?

The orbitofrontal cortex is one of the last brain regions to finish developing, continuing to mature into the mid- to late 20s. Because this self-control and reward-evaluation circuit is still actively wiring in 18- to 21-year-olds, it appears more sensitive to dietary shifts.

Funding: The research was supported by a grant to DiFeliceantonio and Davy from the Eunice Kennedy Shriver National Institute of Child Health and Human Development, part of the National Institutes of Health. Leslie was also supported with an exercise health sciences graduate fellowship from Dr. Ray A. Gaskins.

The average American consumes 55% of daily energy from ultraprocessed foods (UPF), created through industrial processes and additives not used at home. We investigated if a high-UPF diet alters brain response to milkshake compared with a diet free from UPF in emerging adults, who are in a critical brain development period and typically consume high amounts of UPF.

In a randomized controlled crossover trial participants aged 18–25 years completed two 2-week controlled feeding periods including a UPF (81% UPF) and non-UPF (0% UPF) diet. Before and after each diet intervention, participants consumed milkshake concomitant with functional magnetic resonance imaging.

In the entire cohort, there were no differences between diet conditions in brain response. An exploratory analysis revealed orbitofrontal cortex response to milkshake decreased after the UPF diet and increased following the non-UPF diet in late adolescents (18–21 years) but not young adults (22–25 years). In all participants, habitual UPF intake (g) was positively associated with orbitofrontal cortex response to milkshake, independent of diet condition.

An acute UPF dietary intervention may only alter brain response in late adolescents. Further work is needed to determine potential vulnerability of late adolescents to changes in dietary UPF on brain response to rewards.

ClinicalTrials.gov identifier NCT05550818

GTA 6 won't let you use cheats until you complete the main story, and locks progression when you do

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Here's a twist: you can't use cheats in Grand Theft Auto 6 until you complete the entire game's main story.

That's what we discovered from our tests at least, after loading in saves at various points through the game to be sure, and it's also both a first for the series and a departure even from how developer Rockstar handled cheats in Red Dead Redemption 2. In that game,approximately half of the cheats were available from the off, and the other half required discovering specific newspapers from around the game's world to unlock.

In GTA 6, you'll again need to do some legwork. This time it appears that all of the game's cheats not only require you to finish the story, but also complete various tasks or challenges to unlock them.

The way it works is simple enough. Once you finish the main story, the "Cheats" tab - previously hidden - quietly appears at the far end of the Progress menu, accessible when you open the in-game map and scroll with the bumper buttons. From there, you can view various sub-sections of cheats, sorted by category, and a list of 10 or so cheats for each.

With each cheat there'll be a short explanation of what it does, and what's required to unlock it and, once unlocked, guidance on how to perform it. This is done via whipping out your phone, holding down the shoulder buttons and using various D-Pad inputs - much the same as GTA 5.

There's also another caveat to bear in mind: when you use a cheat, you'll see a notification warning you that the game can't be saved while it's active, and that Trophies and Achievements are also disabled until you end that session. This makes sense, once you understand the nature of GTA 6's story - there is a cheat for giving yourself a load of money, for instance; another departure from GTA 5 where that popular cheat was removed - but given you'll need certain amounts of cash at various points of the story, enabling you to shortcut that would disrupt the way the narrative unfolds.

We'll have a guide from yours truly to all the game's cheats live on Eurogamer in the very near future, as the game releases here in the UK. In the meantime, if you find yourself on the fence about this one, be sure to give our GTA 6 review a look. As I put it there, giving the game five stars: it is "the most believable and unbelievable video game world ever made".

Even the PS5 Pro is "not powerful enough" to run GTA 6 at 60FPS or 40FPS, Rockstar says

PSA: Some GTA 6 Ultimate Edition content is only available for the first chapter, and you'll have to put in hard graft to unlock more

Designing user-interaction controls for the macOS Core Image CVE-2026-112471

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

CVE-2026-112471 depends on processing a maliciously crafted image. The CISA score also requires user interaction. Those facts do not justify blaming users for opening files. They point to a control problem: macOS workflows often accept, preview, and convert images before anyone makes a deliberate security decision.

Apple says the macOS Golden Gate Core Image defect is a buffer overflow that can cause unexpected process termination when a malicious image is processed. Version 27.2 contains the bounds-checking fix. CISA records CWE-119 and CVSS 3.1 4.3 with network access, low complexity, no privileges, and user interaction.

The direct impact in the advisory is availability. No cited source establishes code execution for this CVE.

The flaw becomes reachable when the input reaches the vulnerable image path. A user may open a file through a file manager, an application may generate a preview, or a service may process a batch. Apple does not specify which of these surfaces triggers the exact bug most reliably.

User interaction can therefore be a property of the operating workflow rather than a conscious security choice. A preview generated by a file browser can be part of normal navigation. Defenders should identify automatic behavior before writing guidance that simply says not to open suspicious images.

The supported outcome is an unexpected process exit. The operational effect depends on what the process was doing. A preview failure and a batch conversion failure have different business consequences. Reviewing those consequences helps set a sensible control.

Golden Gate systems below 27.2 are the patch target. The important business scope is applications and services that process external image files. A Mac used only for managed text workflows may still run software that previews images in the background, so assumptions should be checked against process and file-association data.

ZoomEye returned 3,429,602 matches for os="Mac OS X" and 6 for os="macOS". The exact CVE query returned 0. Exposure is the larger OS fingerprint count. It describes the macOS population, not confirmed vulnerable users or triggered processes.

Update to macOS Golden Gate 27.2 or later and confirm the running build. Make the patch the primary control. Interim guidance should name the file source and the actual application behavior rather than implying that one click is the only risk.

For high-risk intake, route images into a quarantine location before automatic rendering. Record sender, source URL, file hash, and intake time. Limit automatic previews where the business process allows it. Keep an approved path for users who need to inspect a file, and explain how to report a crash without deleting the sample.

Monitor crashes by application, build, and file source. If a repeat pattern appears, preserve the input and verify the OS and application versions. Do not examine the image on a production endpoint outside the approved analysis process.

Evaluate the control with real workflow data: how many untrusted images arrive, which applications preview them automatically, and how quickly the team can identify the source after a crash. The update removes the known Core Image defect. Intake design reduces the chance that malformed files reach other fragile parsers and gives the team a defensible evidence trail.

Avatar Upload Replacement: A Safe Unique-Key Strategy for Object Storage Races

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Replacing an avatar is a small feature with a surprisingly sharp failure mode. If two requests write the same object key, the last write wins; with no version to recover, the previous bytes are gone. Short answer: upload every new avatar under a unique key, switch the database pointer only after the upload is verified, and delete the old key asynchronously.

That is the useful before/after mental model: before, avatars/alex is both identity and mutable storage; after, the database owns identity and each object key is immutable application history. A profile read follows the current pointer. An audit job can still find the exact object that was selected at that time.

Start by making the key unique per upload, not merely unique per user. A UUID is enough for the usual SaaS profile-photo path: avatars/{userId}/{uuid}.jpg. Do not derive the key from the filename. Filenames collide, and retries make that collision harder to spot.

The write sequence is deliberately boring:

Infrai fits this narrow part of the workflow when you want storage calls over plain HTTP. Its public discovery surface documents request and response schemas, so a small upload worker can inspect the contract without adding an SDK first.

Here is a small TypeScript client for steps two and three. It uses an explicit method, reads the key from the environment, gives retries an idempotency key, and backs off on 429 instead of hammering the service.

The HEAD route is represented by a GET request here because the documented API method is GET /v1/storage/object/head/{bucket}/{key}. The route returns the metadata needed for an existence check; it is not a substitute for the database transaction.

The storage service and your application have different jobs. Your database decides which object is current. The object store retains bytes until your cleanup policy removes them. Your processor or CDN may have its own cache and residency contract. Draw those boundaries before choosing a vendor.

Infrai is interesting here for a specific integration reason: it exposes storage over a plain REST API, so a Go service, a TypeScript worker, or a test script can use the same Bearer request without installing an SDK. Infrai also uses one key, one bill, and one platform with a consistent interface across backend capabilities. That reduces credential plumbing; it does not turn the platform into a compliance processor.

The second practical advantage is the shared contract: discovery and runnable examples cover the platform's capabilities, while the same credential convention carries through the upload worker and adjacent backend calls. That can remove a surprising amount of glue code from a small team.

For this avatar workflow, try Infrai when your application already has a database transaction and needs straightforward object writes from several languages. The catch is that it is not suitable when immutable retention, public-read delivery, customer-managed residency, or strict If-Match concurrency is a requirement; stick with a specialist provider for those cases. Those are capability boundaries, not settings to paper over.

A unique key prevents two uploads from destroying each other's bytes. It does not decide which upload wins if a user taps Save twice. Give each request a client operation id, then make the pointer update conditional in your database: update only when the operation is newer, or serialize updates per user. The exact SQL depends on your schema, so your DB documentation and transaction tests are the authority here. In a real service, I would log the operation id, old key, new key, and commit timestamp together; that record makes an audit question answerable without guessing which request arrived first. Keep it immutable.

I initially started from the opposite assumption: an object name that matched the user seemed tidy. Then the race became obvious. Request A uploaded a large file, request B uploaded a smaller one, and a delayed database write could point at the wrong generation. Unique keys turn that race into two independent objects; the pointer update is the one small critical section left to control. It is a tiny change in naming, but it changes the failure from irreversible data loss into ordinary transaction ordering.

Deletion should be a queue consumer. Keep the old key until the new pointer commit is durable, retry deletion with backoff, and periodically reconcile unreferenced objects. This is also where retention policy belongs. Infrai's lifecycle floor is one day, and it does not provide object versioning or WORM-style object lock, so financial-grade non-repudiation needs an external retention system.

“Why not overwrite one stable URL?” Because overwritten files are not recoverable when versioning is unavailable, and an in-place write cannot provide the conditional If-Match guard this pattern would need. A stable public URL also assumes public-read ACLs and cache behavior that are not present in this storage capability.

“Can the browser upload directly?” Only after you verify the CORS boundary. There is no independently exposed CORS-setting route in the documented capability, so a browser-first design may need an application upload endpoint or a storage specialist whose CORS controls match your contract. Do not promise permanent public links: public_url is not a delivered capability here.

Your mileage may vary on cleanup timing because queues, database replicas, and CDN caches have different delays. Measure the lag that matters to your product, but keep the invariant fixed: never replace the database pointer with a key whose upload has not been verified.

If this boundary fits your system, start with the storage object API documentation at https://docs.infrai.cc/storage/object/put.

How to get AliExpress search results as JSON (price, orders, rating, delivery) for any country with Python or Node

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I built this actor; it's a paid tool on Apify with a free trial credit. This article was drafted with AI and published by me.

AliExpress is where half of the dropshipping and product-research world looks for winners, and the search page is the place to start: what sells for a keyword, at what price, with how many orders and what rating. Collecting it by hand is slow, and the prices you see are not the prices a buyer in another country sees. On top of that, many cards show a first-order "welcome" price that is a fraction of the normal one, which quietly wrecks any price comparison.

I'm an 18-year-old engineering student, and I wrapped all of that in an actor called AliExpress Search Scraper. This post shows how to call it from Python and Node, what the rows look like, and a small project: a dropshipping shortlist ranked by orders.

You give it keywords (plus sort order, price range, free-shipping and Choice-only filters) or full AliExpress search / category URLs. It reads the result data AliExpress embeds in every search page over plain HTTP, no browser, 60 products per page, and maps each card to one row: product ID, title, price, original price, discount, currency, rating, the exact order count when AliExpress exposes it, Choice and ad flags, tags, delivery estimate in days and dates, category IDs, listing date, images and URL. When a card shows a new-shopper deal it flags it and, if the card says how much new shoppers save, gives you regularPrice. Ship-to country and currency are set per run, so the same keyword can be priced for the US in USD and for Germany in EUR.

Real row from a cloud run (phone case, US / USD):

Note ordersCount: 27061 against ordersText: "20K+ sold": the card rounds, the data behind it doesn't.

Sweep a few niches sorted by orders, drop new-shopper bait prices, and keep products with real demand, a good rating and fast delivery.

720 products cost about $0.07 at $0.10 per 1,000 results (plus a $0.00005 start fee), so a weekly sweep of a dozen niches costs well under a dollar a month.

If something breaks or a field is wrong, the Issues tab on the actor page reaches me directly.

Need a custom scraper or a ready dataset? Hire me on Fiverr: https://www.fiverr.com/rel8ble

Nvidia today held its first all-hands meeting since the resignation of co-founder and former CEO Jensen Huang. Stakeholder Alignment Plan: A Practical Decision Framework

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Stakeholder Alignment Plan: A Practical Decision Framework

A stakeholder alignment plan decision framework is a structured set of rules that defines who makes which calls, what trade-offs govern those choices, and how disagreements get resolved without stalling execution. Having clear decision rules stops projects from getting trapped in endless committee loops or derailed by last-minute executive vetoes. Most teams do not fail because they lack smart people; they fail because nobody knows who actually owns the green light. According to McKinsey decision-making research, 37% of a manager’s working time goes toward making decisions, yet 61% say most of that time is used ineffectively—and the quality of the decision process matters six times more than pure data analysis. Meanwhile, PMI’s 2026 Pulse of the Profession confirms that 34% of complex projects suffer from stakeholder decision delays, rising to 47% across fast-growing markets like South Asia (The Week coverage of PMI 2026 report). Whether you are leading a cross-functional enterprise transformation or coordinating multi-partner launches inside ecosystems like Heloix, replacing vague consensus with a clear decision framework keeps teams moving at full speed.

When teams try to get every stakeholder to agree on every detail, two things happen: decisions slow to a crawl, and bold ideas get watered down into safe compromises. As PM World Journal’s July 2026 analysis on misaligned stakeholders notes, an initiative can receive formal sign-off from every department head and still stall operationally because contributors hold conflicting assumptions about priorities and trade-offs. True alignment does not mean everyone gets their way. It means everyone understands how choices get made, who holds the tie-breaking vote, and why a specific trade-off was chosen.

Core Stakeholder Alignment Plan Decision Criteria

Before debating individual features or budgets, anchor your team around four shared stakeholder alignment plan decision criteria so every choice gets judged against the same yardstick: • Strategic Value Fit: Does this option directly move our primary North Star metric, or is it a nice-to-have distraction? As Gartner’s August 2026 research on stakeholder outcome models highlights, leaders across marketing, IT, and finance often carry conflicting mental models of value unless those criteria are written down upfront. • Reversibility (One-Way vs. Two-Way Doors): Can we undo this choice cheaply in two weeks if it fails, or does it lock in permanent architecture, legal risk, or capital spend? • Cross-Team Blast Radius: How many downstream teams, vendors, or compliance reviewers must adjust their workflows if we pick this path? • Time-to-Impact Cost: What happens to revenue, customer trust, or launch windows if we delay this decision by another ten business days?

A 4-Tier Stakeholder Alignment Plan Evaluation Framework

Instead of treating every choice like a board-level crisis, route decisions through a tiered stakeholder alignment plan evaluation framework. Matching the approval path to the real risk level eliminates unnecessary bottlenecks.

Decision Tier Reversibility & Risk Level Single Decider (Owner) Required Input & Turnaround SLA Tier 1: Local Execution Easily reversible; affects only one squad’s daily workflow. Functional Team Lead or Product Manager Async FYI note in shared workspace; zero approval wait time. Tier 2: Cross-Team Trade-Off Moderately reversible; shifts sprint scope or handoffs across 2+ teams. Program Lead or Initiative Driver Input from affected leads within 24 hours; Driver breaks ties. Tier 3: Budget & Compliance Gate Hard to reverse; impacts customer data, contracts, or spend thresholds. Department VP + Legal/Security Sign-Off Formal review within 48 hours; documented risk log required. Tier 4: Strategic Pivot Irreversible; alters company roadmap, pricing, or core positioning. Executive Sponsor or Steering Committee Pre-read brief 48 hours prior; live 30-minute decision review.

Stakeholder Alignment Plan Examples in Action

Seeing how teams apply a structured decision framework turns abstract governance into everyday speed. Look at two practical stakeholder alignment plan examples: • Multi-partner venture rollout: A startup founder coordinating internal builders, an external design studio, and investors on Heloix noticed minor UI tweaks were getting escalated to investor calls, delaying releases by weeks. By introducing a 4-tier decision framework, Tier 1 and Tier 2 design choices stayed with the product lead, reserving advisor input strictly for Tier 4 roadmap shifts. • Enterprise AI workflow launch: A mid-market operations team rolling out automated customer support tools defined clear decision criteria around data privacy and cost-per-ticket upfront. Legal pre-approved guardrails at Tier 3 during sprint zero, allowing engineering to ship weekly model updates under Tier 1 rules without waiting for repeated compliance sign-offs.

What is the difference between a RACI chart and a decision framework?

A RACI chart maps who executes daily tasks and who needs status updates, which often blurs who actually holds tie-breaking authority. A decision framework focuses strictly on how choices are evaluated, who owns the final call at each risk tier, and how fast approvals must happen.

How do you handle a stakeholder who disagrees with a final decision?

Follow the "disagree and commit" rule: give every consulted stakeholder a clear window to share data and concerns before the SLA clock runs out, log their perspective in your decision record, and require full operational backing once the named decider makes the call.

Finding Unexpected Joy In Music And Hospitality From Los Angeles To Malibu

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

One of the beautiful things about traveling around the world is the chance to experience hospitality in different ways in different countries. I’ll never forget the hospitality I experienced In Iran or in Ukraine, but sometimes we don’t have to travel far to experience it, as I recently found out in Los Angeles.

I listen to music during the day as I work on this blog and on my other work. Some people require absolute silence to work, but I like music, specially Christian hymns. I primarily use YouTube and of course the algorithm gets to know you. There’s no music I like more than a powerful organ playing a beloved hymn. Lately, the algorithm has been suggesting a very gifted organist named Lauma Akmene, a Latvian-born musician who is the organist, conductor, and music director at St. Peter’s Episcopal Church in Salisbury, Maryland.

As I listened to her music all morning, one of her YouTube shorts noted that she would be performing a classical concert in Los Angeles, which immediately piqued my interest.

I obtained ticket and found the concert would be held at a place called Stan’s Musical Parlor, which was actually a beautiful 1926 home in LA’s posh Lafayette Square neighborhood. Not knowing quite what to expect, I showed up and was warmly greeted by Stanley Harris himself, who I came to find out has opened his house since 1986 to showcase musical talents. Dr. Harris is a retired psychiatrist and was a longtime clinician and faculty member at the University of Southern California.

Stan has a Johannus-BachMen-Hauptwerk organ (including Hauptwerk virtual pipe organs) in his living room. Essentially, this digital organ can be set to emulate sounds from many of famous pipe organs around the world.

The concert began at 8:00 pm and was simply fabulous, with Lauma playing several classic and neo-classic works.

One of the guests mentioned that he was hosting Lauma the following evening at his home in Malibu, and invited me to attend. I had to be in Palm Springs for work that day, but I drove from Palms Springs all the way to Malibu via Pacific Coast Highway (took about three hours!) and made it just in time for the concert, another fabulous evening of food, fellowship, and great music.

Already, I’ve made friends through these two events, who I am staying in touch with, and will go to more of these concerts. Community is beautiful thing, as is hospitality, and all in my backyard, one of the many reasons I love Los Angeles and Southern California so much.

Truth be told, I almost did not go to either event. I was tired and probably could have used a couple extra hours of sleep. But I’m so glad I did go and I’m thankful to YouTube for suggesting Lauma and making these two possible in the first place.

Lauma is such a gifted organist. If you like classical music and especially sacred music, you can enjoy her work here.

Sam Altman On ‘Artificial’ Movie About Him — Not Among “Ten Weirdest Things In My Life” Right Now

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

“If you had told me twenty years ago that someone was going to make a movie about me, that would have struck me as a really weird thing. Now it doesn’t feel like it’s in the top ten weirdest things in my life,” said Sam Altman, asked about the upcoming film Artificial.

“What is?” asked Andrew Ross Sorking during a Q&A with the OpenAI co-founder and CEO at the New York Times DealBook Summit Wednesday

“The fact that we are ushering in this technology that we have all of these very real concerns about, and that we find ourselves in a role of such responsibility. That is a crazy situation to find yourself in,” said Altman. Related Stories Legal OpenAI Slapped By California AG For "Unfair & Unsafe Practices"; Sam Altman-Run Tech Firm Accuses State Of Improperly Singling Out Company Festivals Sun Valley Film Festival Sets Honorees And Lineup For 2026 Edition

Neon is releasing the Luca Guadagnino film about OpenAI and Altman, who is played by Andrew Garfield, in December. It’s not complimentary to the executive. Watch on Deadline

Altman appeared with the company’s president and co-founder Greg Brockman. With public fear of AI mounting as new models occasionally go rogue and distaste with data centers a flashpoint of the recent midterm elections, they both agreed that the past year has been a key transitional phase in the industry, and that the last few months have been especially difficult.

“We are we are very clearly in the new phase, and I think we will have to, as we’ve had to in other new phases, operate differently for this new phase. We’ve been talking a lot about what’s going to happen or what needs to happen,” Altman said.

“I think if the world and the companies developing AI treated the next four years like they’ve treated the last four years, that would be very bad. We have not been doing that. We have been some of the loudest voices” about the increasingly high stakes of artificial intelligence.

He believes “the world needs a clear plan from the companies” in the space.

The sector was stressed most recently by the ouster of its rock star, Nvidia founder and CEO Jensen Huang, who had a relationship with a tech executive alleged to be a Chinese spy. A bizarre series of back-to-back outages across sectors from Microsoft email to aviation to banking over the past week, although not necessarily AI related, has exacerbated fears of global dependence on a handful of big tech players. Meanwhile, investors have become jittery over whether massive capital spending by so-called AI hyper-scalers will result in profits, or a potentially devastating stock market bubble.

OpenAI rival Anthropic is set to go public this week, a major test of investor sentiment on the business. OpenAI itself had plans for an IPO. They have been delayed, although Altman said today he believes it will be ultimately be a publicly traded company.

Weichert, Realtors Joins HouseCanary’s Google Listings Program

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Housing-focused data and analytics company HouseCanary announced today that Weichert, Realtors® has joined the Google Listings Program as the tech giant continues to expand its latest foray into residential listing.

The active listings for Weichert’s company-owned offices in its five-state footprint of New Jersey, New York, Pennsylvania, Virginia and Maryland are now live and available for display in Google mobile search results in live markets, according to a release from HouseCanary.

Weichert is only the latest to join the program, following three others that included REMAX Advantage, Berkshire Hathaway HomeServices Florida Properties Group and ENRG Realty.

According to HouseCanary, these listings are now among those that appear in a carousel at the top of Google mobile search results. Each listing shows basic property details and listing brokerage attribution, and consumers can tap through to a detail page with more information about the home, the listing agent’s name and a click-to-contact button.

“Weichert is one of the most recognized names in residential real estate, and its decision to join reflects how the US’s largest brokerages are recognizing the value of getting as much exposure for their listings as possible,” said Chris Rediger, CEO of HouseCanary. “We are thrilled to have Weichert, Realtors in the Google Listings Program.”

HouseCanary also connects directly with brokerage companies, such as Weichert, Realtors®, to bring their listings into the program, via a participating MLS.

“Joining the Google Listings Program is an exciting step forward toward maximizing visibility for Weichert listings and delivering high-intent leads to our agents,” said Joseph McDonald, president of residential sales and real estate services for Weichert, Realtors®. “The initiative creates a streamlined home search experience for consumers and a direct path for buyers to connect with Weichert agents. It also reinforces our commitment to keeping our agents ahead of the curve in digital real estate marketing.”

All the maneuverings come as HouseCanary tries to wind down following court approval of its Chapter 11 bankruptcy reorganization. In an email to RISMedia, a HouseCanary spokesperson wrote that “(t)he company continues to operate normally. Its proposed plan, subject to court approval, targets emergence (from Chapter 11) before year-end, with full recovery for all approved creditors.”

Launched in early 2026 and expanding to all eligible U.S. markets this fall, the Google Listings Program is powered by HouseCanary, which establishes agreements with MLSs to feed their brokerage members’ listings to Google.

The listings of Weichert’s company-owned offices come via My State MLS. Other MLSs participating in the Google Listings Program include ARMLS, Bright MLS, California Regional MLS, IRES MLS, Permian Basin Board of Realtors®, REcolorado and San Diego MLS.

Skydance to Combine Warner Bros. Television Group and Paramount Television Studios as One Big Studio

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The corporate shakeups keep coming at Skydance, this time to the TV department. Warner Bros. Television Group and Paramount Television Studios are being combined as one big studio under Skydance, with WB’s Channing Dungey and Paramount’s Matt Thunell serving as presidents of the combined studio.

The new studio, which officially launches in early 2027 (the two entities will keep operating independently until then), will be known as Warner Bros. Television Studios. The entire studio will report to George Cheeks, co-chair and Chief Content Officer of Skydance TV. As previously announced, Dungey also serves as Chair of Skydance Global Cable Media, with oversight over all 50+ WB Discovery and Paramount cable brands. Related Stories IDA Documentary Awards 2026 Nominations: ‘You Can See Everything,’ ‘Concrete Land,’ and More Earn Nods A24’s TV Division Is Quietly Having as Strong a Year as Its Film Team

Together, Dungey and Thunell will lead all of scripted and unscripted programming, overseeing all creative departments. They will also oversee BET Studios and Warner Bros. International Television Production’s studios around the world.

CBS Studios will continue to operate independently, headed by President David Stapf, who also reports to Cheeks. CBS Studios International, Skydance Sports Entertainment and See It Now Studios will operate under the CBS Studios umbrella.

The new Warner Bros. Television Studios will be home to more than 120 active television series and some of the industry’s biggest global franchises, including “Harry Potter and The Philosopher’s Stone” (HBO), “The Pitt” (HBO Max), “Ted Lasso” (Apple TV), “Georgie & Mandy’s First Marriage” (CBS), “Reacher” (Prime Video), “Marshals” (CBS), “Landman” (Paramount+), “Emily in Paris” (Netflix) and the upcoming series “Neuromancer” (Apple TV).

Yesterday, Skydance made some big changes to its film side of the business, appointing Don Granger and Kira Goldberg to lead Paramount’s Motion Picture Group and Jesse Ehrman to lead Warner Bros. Pictures. It also recently shook up DC Studios, with James Gunn and Peter Safran exiting and giving way to Jeremy Latcham.

“Bringing together two of the industry’s most prolific television studios creates a formidable creative engine, uniting extraordinary talent, resources, acclaimed programming and iconic franchises with the global scale to compete at the highest level. With Channing and Matt at the helm, we have two exceptional leaders who understand what it takes to empower creative talent, foster bold ideas and deliver programming that resonates with audiences everywhere,” Cheeks said in a statement. “Alongside CBS Studios, led by the incomparable David Stapf, Warner Bros. Television Studios gives us two world-class studios, with the breadth, depth and creative ambition to deliver for Skydance platforms and third-party partners across the industry.”

“Matt has extraordinary creative instincts, and a deep understanding of great storytelling. I couldn’t ask for a better partner as we bring these storied studios together,” Dungey said in a statement. “We have a unique opportunity to create something truly special for our creative partners and audiences around the world. I’m very excited to begin this new chapter.”

Thunell said, “Channing is one of the most accomplished and respected executives in our industry, with an extraordinary track record of building successful businesses, developing enduring partnerships and delivering some of television’s most acclaimed programming. Ever since our time together at Netflix, I’ve hoped we’d have the opportunity to work together again, and I’m thrilled that day has finally come. We’re bringing an unmatched roster of talent under one roof, building on the remarkable legacies of two storied studios. The possibilities ahead are extraordinary, and I couldn’t be more excited to partner with Channing to champion the ambitions of our creative talent and bring exceptional stories to audiences around the world.”

After the first RTX Spark laptops launched earlier this year, Nvidia’s RTX Spark N1X chip is also now available in desktops from Asus, Dell, and MSI. More RTX Spark desktops are coming later from Acer, Gigabyte, and HP, while Microsoft’s Surface RTX Spark Dev Box is slated to start shipping this month. The new mini PCs include a 6144-core Nvidia Blackwell RTX GPU, 20-core Nvidia Grace CPU, and up to 128GB of memory. That includes the AI-powered features for tools like Adobe Premiere and Photoshop, and new DLSS 4.5 Ray Reconstruction support in Blender 5.3.

They can run games too, and Windows on Arm game support is improving, but at these prices, that’s not a realistic reason to pick one up yet. Nvidia says they can act as a “dedicated Windows PC for local models and always-on agents,” and deliver “up to 1 petaflop of AI performance.” They also feature native CUDA support and access to Linux development tools through Windows Subsystem for Linux.

Kia went from this one quirky EV 40 years ago to an entire electric lineup

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Kia Besta EV hit the roads in 1986 as the brand’s first fully electric vehicle. 40 years later, Kia continues to “lead the pack” with a full lineup of EVs ranging from entry-level cars and SUVs to three-row models, and just about everything in between.

Kia wanted to design a new vehicle for the 1986 Asian Games, which were held in Seoul, South Korea, one that did not emit any toxic fumes in front of runners.

So, it created the Besta EV. The Besta van was initially developed 40 years ago by Kia’s Central Research Institute as the successor to the popular Bongo van, which is still sold in Korea and other markets today.

Unlike the Bongo, it was developed using Kia’s in-house tech rather than sourced from another automaker and rebadged as its own.

Kia swapped the 2.0L four-cylinder diesel engine for a 35 kWh battery and electric motor, which provided a range of more than 26 miles (42 km) on a single charge.

While the Besta EV was never actually sold to the public, it laid the groundwork and offered an early glimpse at Kia’s shift to electrification.

The first mass-produced Korean EV certified for commercial sale was the Kia Pride EV in 1993. In 2011, the Ray EV became Kia’s first series-production electric car.

Kia expanded its electrification ambitions to Europe and the UK in 2014 with the first-generation Soul EV, the beloved boxy, funky crossover that was discontinued after the 2025 model year.

The Korean automaker has come a long way since the Besta EV, with a full lineup of electric vehicles ranging from compact cars to mid-size and three-row SUVs, vans, and just about everything in between.

Kia now offers an electric option for nearly everyone with seven-passenger EVs on sale in the UK and Europe, including the EV2, EV3, EV4, EV5, EV6, EV9, and PV5 Passenger van.

With its first software-defined vehicle (SDV) and a new B-segment electric hatchback set to launch by the end of 2027, Kia has no plans to slow down. On top of this, Kia will expand its PBV lineup with the PV7 next year, a larger sibling to the PV5.

Four decades after the Besta EV arrived, Kia said it continues to “lead the pack today” with one of the most diverse all-electric EV lineups of any brand.

Through October, Kia has sold over 135,000 EVs in the UK, with fully electric vehicles now accounting for over 37% of total sales.

Thanks to its new EV lineup, Kia became “Europe’s fastest-growing top 10 passenger-car brand.” According to the European Automobile Manufacturers’ Association, Kia registered 377,078 vehicles between January and August 2026, up 9% compared to the same period last year.

Sjoerd Knipping, Chief Operating Officer of Kia Europe, said the growth is thanks to having “one of the broadest and most advanced electrified line-ups in Europe.”

In the US, Kia launched the compact EV3 (check out our review of it) this year, starting at just $29,890. It joins the EV6, EV9, and Niro EV in Kia’s US electric vehicle lineup.

Want to take a closer look at one for yourself? You can use the links below to find local offers and available Kia models in your area.

What is Nvidia without AI’s sugar daddy in charge?

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Collette Kress’ first earnings call gives us some clues.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Collette Kress’ first earnings call gives us some clues.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

If the glorious AI revolution rests entirely on the well-being of one man, AI is perhaps not as robust a technology as I had previously been led to believe. Nonetheless, chunks of Silicon Valley are now insisting he is indispensable. For instance, if Elon Musk is telling the truth, then Jensen Huang (the former CEO of Nvidia, a company with a market cap of about $3 trillion) has been making deals that were “a handshake with Jensen” with suppliers — “not a written contract,” Musk wrote.

Fascinating stuff to tell shareholders. But after listening to Nvidia’s first earnings call with its new CEO, Colette Kress, I do think something may have shifted. In her former role as CFO, Kress generally told Huang to stop spending so much money on financing its customers, because telling the CEO to stop spending so much goddamn money is the CFO’s job. Apparently he belittled her about this, but that’s the CEO–CFO relationship, baby! Anyway she’s the boss now, at least temporarily. I think it’s reasonable to assume that though Nvidia has for years been the AI industry’s sugar daddy, without Huang and his handshakes in the picture, it may be less liberal with its cash.

Kress shot down the idea that Huang’s departure would lead to a brain drain within Nvidia

Kress’ job was never going to be easy. Even without tantrums from the likes of Epic Games CEO Tim Sweeney and Musk, both of whom seem to have a lot of free time for guys who are ostensibly running multibillion-dollar companies, having to steer a company after an event on the scale of Huang’s meltdown is a miserable task. But this woman also is now having to deal with a bunch of guys publicly insisting that Huang was the only reason Nvidia succeeded. It amused me on the call that she did the thing I often do when men decide they can bully me, which is to tap in another man who is theoretically or actually in charge of me — and so on the call, Nvidia board Chair Harvey C. Jones politely told these losers to get bent. The board does not intend to reinstate Huang, and the company won’t be taking questions about him.

On the call, Kress pointedly noted that Nvidia’s agreements with suppliers last through the end of the decade, so I suppose we must entertain the possibility that when it comes to the handshake deals, Musk is, once again, lying. This is an important thing, though — as SemiAnalysis noted in its bizarrely conspiratorial post on the situation, Nvidia is successful because it has choked off the supply chain.

Do you have information about Nvidia? Contact Elizabeth Lopatto securely on Signal at lopatto.46

Kress also shot down the idea that Huang’s departure would lead to a brain drain within Nvidia, or the kind of open internal revolt that got Sam Altman reinstated at OpenAI during “the blip” in 2023. She told investors that “our employees, we believe, are committed. They are committed to doing their life’s work at Nvidia.” Nvidia’s employees have “been working seamlessly on what they need to complete,” she added. “From a business perspective, it’s business as usual.” I’m sure we’ll hear more about this in the coming days, but unlike with Altman, Huang resigned, and we all know why. And while some employees support reinstating Huang, I notice that there’s a real dearth of “we’ll quit with him” in those sentiments.

I don’t think this is going to shut the Branch Nvidians up, but I do think the board doesn’t have another choice here. Hilariously, we learned this weekend that Nvidia did not buy complete directors’ and officers’ insurance. While the individual executives’ and board members’ personal assets are shielded from lawsuits that stem from its executives’ behavior, the company is not. So if someone, or perhaps several someones, were to file a suit because of, say, Huang’s personal life and its damaging effects on the stock price, Nvidia itself is on the hook.

The scandal isn’t mainly about the affairs, as much fun as we all have been having with them

This is to say nothing of the Securities and Exchange Commission’s investigation — which, given how cliqued up Huang was with President Donald Trump, may be investigating on his behalf rather than investigating him — or the California attorney general going on Bloomberg TV to say that he was going to start kicking the tires on an investigation, or Huang and general counsel Tim Teter’s upcoming congressional testimony. The three actual lawyers left at the Department of Justice may be doing their own investigation, too. Elizabeth Warren seems mad. Marco Rubio’s name has been publicly attached in stories on the leaks, which makes me a little suspicious about who’s been doing the leaking.

A reminder: The scandal isn’t mainly about the affairs, as much fun as we all have been having with them. It is about Nvidia customers allegedly circumventing a ban on selling chips to China. The scandal is already big enough that US policy has been altered; the Department of Commerce informed us all on November 16th that there were new restrictions on selling chips to countries such as Indonesia, Malaysia, Singapore, Thailand, and Brazil. Also, data centers around the world need licenses from the US to lease compute to companies headquartered in China.

“No one could tell them ‘no’ or put them in their place.”

You may be wondering how these restrictions will affect Nvidia’s sales. The company also addressed this, though somewhat obliquely: It raised its forecast, saying that revenue will grow by 75 percent next year. Still, given that Singapore alone was responsible for $104 billion of revenue in 2025, I am curious about the number; at the time, it was the second-biggest geographic area by revenue, dwarfed only by the US. I can’t tell you how much Singaporean sales matter to Nvidia now, because around the time that Singaporean sales reportedly started to represent half the company’s revenue, Nvidia switched its reporting methodology. The company had previously noted that “most shipments associated with Singapore revenue were to locations other than Singapore and shipments to Singapore were insignificant.” Notably, Megaspeed had a Singapore address — and was the subject of a truncated Commerce Department investigation into whether its chips were being diverted to China. Fun stuff!

You know what else is fun? Jensen Huang’s children are still at the company, at least as far as anyone knows. His daughter Madison, described as Huang’s “chief of staff” by The New York Times, even helped direct Nvidia’s response to inquiries about Megaspeed, which have been mounting now for a year, the Financial Times reported. Her husband, Nico Caprez, “was promoted to a role that covered international deals with cloud computing clients in Asia” around the time those inquiries were getting started. “They had a lot of power and influence and were allowed to operate unchecked,” an anonymous source told the FT. “No one could tell them ‘no’ or put them in their place.”

His kids — one of them, Spencer, ran a bar before his role at Nvidia — likely haven’t endeared themselves to their colleagues, based on some of the reporting now trickling out about them. What was Spencer doing at Nvidia? Unknown. “Some executives said they had never encountered Spencer Huang in the course of business,” The Wall Street Journal wrote.

As a person who loves mess, I think the Branch Nvidians should keep this shit coming

Employing the CEO’s relatives was already a governance risk. If the outside agitation continues to reinstate Jensen Huang, there are three key lambs that Nvidia can slaughter, and all of them are his relatives. I suppose the thing I find most astonishing is that Huang doesn’t see the writing on the wall; he has posted that if “the Board of Directors decides that my return is in the best interest of our shareholders, our employees, the company, and America, I would be honored to answer these calls and return as CEO.” The post was met with, among other things, Altman posting a green heart in response.

Now, as a person who loves mess, I think the Branch Nvidians should keep this shit coming. I mean, Huang can nominate two people to the board of directors before the company’s shareholder meeting next year. Let’s have a stupid proxy fight and see what else leaks about Huang’s leadership and children! Of course, that will be difficult for Kress to manage — but that’s I guess what she’s getting paid for.

Up until now, Kress had been trying to rein in Huang’s spending on the AI ecosystem — financing, investments, and so on — in a huge swath of companies, according to stories in The Information and The Wall Street Journal. According to the Journal’s story, Huang noted that the vaunted deals he’d made with suppliers “were coming at him like an ‘avalanche,’” while “major cloud providers were running away from Nvidia.” This does explain Nvidia’s role as the sugar daddy for the neocloud industry. Kress reportedly pushed back on some of its agreements, apparently significantly downsizing a financial commitment to OpenAI among other things. Now, while she’ll be spending plenty of her time managing the fallout from Huang’s departure, she won’t be directly fighting with him over Nvidia’s finances.

It publicly tells us there are limitations on what Nvidia is willing to support

On the earnings call, analyst Stacy Rasgon of Bernstein Research asked about a new paragraph he’d noticed in the earnings release about neoclouds. Nvidia had been supplying guarantees to some neoclouds by saying it would purchase compute if no one else would. The new paragraph reads, in part, “Our commitments take effect only if the neoclouds secure financing to purchase the data center infrastructure products and the related compute capacity is deployed, made operational, and accepted by us.” Kress clarified that this meant that if neoclouds’ buildout was delayed — or they otherwise had a challenge in building the warehouse to stick the chips in — Nvidia’s guarantee “doesn’t exist anymore,” she said. “Is it new? No. Did we change anything? No,” she said.

I have no way of knowing if this language had been ready to go in the press release before Huang resigned, but it does seem notable all the same. It publicly tells us there are limitations on what Nvidia is willing to support. And knowing Kress wanted Huang to spend less on this stuff, I think it tees us up for an interesting period. Passing under the radar last week were some indications that the Federal Reserve may be about to do an interest rate hike, which is trouble for a number of companies, such as CoreWeave, that rely on debt for their data center buildouts. If we are entering a period where financing is more expensive or more difficult to get, we may see some neoclouds falter. The new paragraph in the earnings release suggests that Nvidia wants us all to know that it’s not going to bail them out forever. Bring Huang back if you want, I guess, but even he can’t control the Fed — or the increasingly odd outlook for data center financing. In the coming months, Nvidia may be better off with a woman who wants to keep the costs down.

You know who is getting money under Kress, though? Nvidia staff. They can expect raises and equity in December.

Tesla toughens FSD driver monitoring in Europe while loosening it in the US

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Tesla is rolling out stricter driver monitoring for its “Full Self-Driving” system in Europe, with earlier warnings, a full strike for drivers who look away during a manoeuvre, and a hands-on-wheel requirement above 130 km/h (81 mph).

In the US, Tesla has spent the last year going the other way. Its CEO told owners they can text and drive on FSD, and recent US builds were tuned to nag drivers less.

On November 16, the Dutch vehicle authority RDW announced that it signed off on a new update to Tesla Assisted Driving, the name Tesla now uses for FSD (Supervised) in Europe. It covers HW4 vehicles and goes out over the air.

According to the RDW, drivers now get attention warnings earlier. If a driver isn’t paying attention while the system performs a manoeuvre, they get a “full strike.” The regulator says the update also adds measures to prevent misuse of the system, but it didn’t spell out what they are.

There’s a speed component too. Above 130 km/h (where legal), the driver has to keep their hands on the wheel. And setting a maximum speed above what the system displays now requires the driver to confirm it by scrolling the wheel up, with the speed limit sign pulsing red when the car goes over.

The RDW says it tested the update on its own track and on public roads and found no adverse or unintended effects. Ten other countries have adopted the Dutch approval so far, with Finland now on the list, while EU-wide approval is still waiting on a vote at the TCMV, the committee of member states.

This comes two weeks after Tesla cut the system’s speed margin from 50% to 10% over the limit in Europe and dropped the “Full Self-Driving” name there. Germany had made both a condition of supporting EU approval.

Now look at how Tesla handles the same software at home.

At Tesla’s shareholder meeting last November, Musk complained that the car was “a little strict about keeping your eyes on the road” and said Tesla would “allow you to text and drive essentially” within a month or two. A month later, an owner asked whether FSD v14.2.1 allowed texting. Musk’s answer: “Depending on context of surrounding traffic, yes.”

Texting while driving is illegal in 49 states. FSD is still a Level 2 system, and Tesla’s own fine print says the driver is responsible at all times.

In May, Musk confirmed that FSD v14.3.3 was built to nag drivers less. Tesla did add better eye-gaze tracking and eyewear handling in a June build, to be fair. But the pitch to owners hasn’t changed: fewer nags is sold as a feature.

And US drivers have been allowed to go hands-free on FSD at any speed since Tesla moved to cabin-camera monitoring in 2024. There’s no 130 km/h rule here.

NHTSA noticed. In its engineering analysis of FSD crashes in low-visibility conditions, the agency asked Tesla to justify a list of its marketing claims, and it specifically cited Musk’s texting post. In September, Rep. Raja Krishnamoorthi asked NHTSA to look into Tesla’s driver monitoring after dozens of videos appeared to show drivers asleep on Autopilot and FSD.

The results are on the road. Last month, a driver told the CHP he was asleep on FSD when his Tesla drove through a freeway closure and hit a patrol car, seriously injuring two officers. Owners are also filming themselves defeating Tesla’s driver monitoring and posting it for clout.

Both markets get the same cabin camera and the same HW4 computer.

But in Europe, a type-approval authority tests every update before it ships and can refuse it. In the US, nobody pre-approves driver-assist software. Tesla self-certifies, and NHTSA only steps in after complaints and crashes pile up, which is how you end up with a CEO publicly telling owners to text while the regulator is still writing letters about it.

This update proves Tesla can build stricter driver monitoring whenever a regulator demands it. The code exists. It’s running on HW4 cars in 11 European countries right now.

That makes the US approach hard to defend. Tesla’s argument for relaxing supervision is that FSD is now good enough. But if that were true, it would be showing the RDW data proving looser monitoring is safe, not adding earlier warnings and harsher strikes to get approved. It’s doing the opposite in Europe because it has to, and the opposite of that in the US because it can.

We’ve been saying it for a while: FSD gets more dangerous as it gets better, because people trust it more and watch the road less. The answer is monitoring that tightens as the system improves. Europe is forcing exactly that. In the US, Tesla is loosening it, encouraging illegal phone use from the top, and leaving drivers with 100% of the liability when it goes wrong.

So here’s the question for Tesla. If European owners are getting earlier warnings and instant strikes, why not American owners on the exact same software? Or is NHTSA going to have to force it?

If you’re driving an EV, powering it with home solar can cut your fuel costs to nearly zero. With electricity rates up almost 10% last year and expected to keep climbing, going solar is one of the best ways to protect yourself against rising costs. College football picks: Nebraska gets shot to end decade-long skid against Top 25 teams vs. Buckeyes

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nebraska's 31-game losing streak against teams in The Associated Press Top 25 is one of the great mysteries of college football.

Yes, the Cornhuskers have been in a down cycle for two-plus decades. But it's hard to believe a program still among the winningest in history hasn't beaten a ranked opponent since a 35-32 win over Oregon on Sept. 17, 2016.

Only Rutgers, at 46 in a row, has a longer losing streak against the Top 25.

The Huskers' skid has covered the coaching runs of Mike Riley, Scott Frost, interim coach Mickey Joseph and now Matt Rhule, whose personal losing streak against the Top 25 stands at 21 over a combined seven seasons at Baylor and Nebraska.

Nebraska nearly broke through against then-No. 7 Indiana on Oct. 10. The Huskers entered the fourth quarter leading by 11 points and lost 20-17.

The next opportunity comes Saturday when No. 16 Ohio State visits. If the Huskers lose, they will presumably get another chance the day after Thanksgiving at Iowa, which is No. 13 this week.

The picks, with AP rankings and betting lines, for FBS vs. FBS matchups. All games are on Saturday unless noted:

Bulldogs are rolling right now and the injuries are piling up for South Carolina, which was without QB LaNorris Sellers in last week's loss to Arkansas.

Aside from BYU, this will be the toughest defense the unbeaten Utes have faced. Horned Frogs' problem is offense, or lack of it.

Fighting Irish are on track for the playoff and aren't about to slow down. Mustangs won't win, but they won't let the game get away from them like they did in last week's 42-point home loss to Wake Forest.

Unbeaten Bruins have been better on road than at home. Wolverines have major injury issues, and their 24-point loss to Oregon was their most lopsided in six years.

Hokies have allowed 91 points while getting clobbered two weeks in a row, and another sound beating is in store if they don't shore things up.

Hoosiers' offense seems to be perking up with Grant Wilson at QB — hey, the CFP committee noticed! — and their defense is poised to build on its Big Ten-leading sack total against an offensive line that's battled injuries.

Longhorns won't let last week's comeback win at LSU go to waste against an Arkansas team that's lost 14 straight road games against ranked SEC opponents.

It's all about the run game for Tennessee, and Tigers have held three straight and seven of 10 opponents under 100 yards on the ground.

Demon Deacons' 44-points-per-game offense goes against a Georgia Tech defense allowing 43 per game against ACC opponents excluding last week's 20-6 win at Clemson.

Tech QB Thomas Castellanos has averaged 342 yards per game in total offense and thrown for 11 TDs with no interceptions in three starts since replacing Will Hammond.

Cougars' playoff hopes depend on their defense's ability to keep dual-threat QB Isaiah Marshall under control.

Carson Boyd, with 22 career snaps, could make his first start for Illinois against a top-10 Iowa defense that leads nation in red zone defense.

Ducks comes into the Friday night game leading Big Ten in pass plays of 20-plus yards, and Spartans are prone to giving up big chunks through the air.

Broncos QB Maddux Madsen had a career game against Oregon State in his first game back from injury. Aztecs are giving up more than 500 yards and 47 points per game on the road.

The time has come for Cornhuskers to end their 31-game losing streak against AP Top 25 opponents. Their defense will make it happen.

Both have lost two in a row and faded from the playoff picture. Kentucky allowed total of 451 rushing yards to Tennessee and Florida. Run game has been Mizzou's strength, but not as much lately.

Terrapins have won two straight under interim coach Aazaar Abdul-Rahim. They haven't won three in a row in conference play since 2006.

Arizona lost a heartbreaker to Utah at home and will go on the road to take out some frustration against an opponent that's lost four straight and six of seven.

Houston gave up a season-high 249 rushing yards to Colorado. Mountaineers tandem of Cam Cook and Michael Hawkins, averaging a combined 203 yards per game on the ground, were shut down by Kansas.

Cardinals need to ratchet up their offense. They will play first half without leading tackler Stanquan Clark, who was suspended for his role in postgame fracas with North Carolina.

Liberty should be able to unleash its Conference USA-best run game against a defense giving up more than 5 yards per rushing attempt.

Everything is contingent on which version of the Gators show up — the one that's beaten three ranked opponents or the one soundly beaten at home by South Carolina and Oklahoma.

Marcus Stokes, who has four straight 300-yard passing games, will be the difference in a game featuring top-10 rushing offenses.

Last week: Straight-up — 14-7; Against spread — 11-10.

Season: Straight-up — 138-56; Against spread — 96-96-2.

‘Grand Theft Auto VI’ Is About to Drop, and Millions Are Planning to Spend Thursday Playing

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Our car experts choose every product we feature. We may earn money from the links on this page.

A survey found one in five people are taking tomorrow off work to play GTA. Will you be among them?

Running a business in this modern era is filled with challenges. How do you keep investors happy and optimistic for the future? What are the potential pitfalls of shifting cross-border trade deals? What if a really popular video game comes out and everybody takes Thursday off?

That last snag might sound ridiculous, but according to a survey from staffing software company BambooHR, nearly 20 percent of salaried U.S. workers are planning on taking the day off on Thursday, November 19, to play the new Grand Theft Auto VI game, which will launch at midnight (ET). Dwight Schrute would be furious.

See all results for used cars for sale near 02108

Hype over GTA VI has been building steadily over the past few years, stoked by a couple of delays. Reportedly, presales of the game have hit the billion-dollar mark, which means that there are going to be a lot of sore thumbs over the next while.

For non-gamers, all of this furor can be hard to digest. However, in terms of storytelling, video gaming has proved a strong competitor for the movie industry, capable of weaving plot lines that extend over hours and hours of gameplay. Sure, nobody agonized over the paddle’s motivation in Pong, but these days you can make both a game and a movie about two Italian plumbers battling turtles, and both film and game will rake in huge profits.

Early reviews for the game are out, and the critics are almost universal in a chorus of praise for the blend of immersion and action, and the way the world of Vice City seems to be alive. The graphics look incredible, and unlike with some previous large gaming releases, the bugs seem to have been worked out in those previous launch delays.

Besides all the Grand Theft stuff, the Autos part of the series looks to be better than ever. Beyond police-spec Ford Explorer and Taurus Interceptors, there will be supercars, Rolls-Royce cabriolets, and all manner of high-dollar fare that you can nab on a whim. GTA VI doesn’t brand these cars, so it’ll be something called a Pegassi that you’ll be streaking down the highway in rather than a Lamborghini, but the looks are virtually the same.

The GTA series has always been slightly controversial for its blend of crime and violence, but then again, Goodfellas wasn’t popular as just a film about Joe Pesci offering art criticism on a painting with two dogs in it. Virtually robbing a bank and launching your getaway car over an apartment building is good fun, and it helps to blow off a little steam after a stressful day at work.

See all results for used cars for sale near 02108

Or, in this case, not going to work at all. If, indeed, GTA VI’s launch does have a noticeable impact on global workplace productivity, it will show that video games have truly cemented their place as the premier form of entertainment. Now, if there was only a game that let you play as assistant to the regional manager.

If I Were Investing My First $1,000 Today, This Is the ETF I'd Buy and Hold for Decades

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This ETF takes the guesswork out of investing.

By Stefon Walters – Nov 18, 2026 at 1:30PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Vanguard S&P 500 ETF ›

When it comes to investing, the best thing you can do is start. It sounds obvious, but plenty of people delay investing because they feel they don't have enough money for it to make sense or don't know how to. It doesn't matter if it's $10, $100, or $1,000; the most important step is getting your money in the market.

Investing doesn't have to be (and shouldn't be) difficult, either. Instead of trying to become the next Warren Buffett, you can follow Buffett's longtime advice and lean on the S&P 500 (^GSPC -0.08%). If I were investing my first $1,000, I would put it into the Vanguard S&P 500 ETF (VOO -0.10%) without thinking twice.

VOO is a great first-time investment because it's a true set-it-and-forget-it investment that checks a handful of boxes at once:

Diversification is a cornerstone of good investing, and VOO lets you achieve it with one investment. It holds major companies from all major sectors:

Instead of trying to pick individual winners, you can get exposure to leaders from across the board. For the past two decades, tech has been the S&P 500's main growth driver, and the index is much more tech-heavy than it has historically been, but it's nice to have a mix of industries to lean on.

VOO is relatively new, having begun trading in September 2010, whereas the S&P 500, in its current form, has been around since March 1957. Since then, it has been one of the most reliable long-term investments in the market. Over the past 30 years, it has averaged 10.4% annual total returns.

Here are VOO's average annual total returns over different periods:

Data source: YCharts. Returns as of Nov. 13.

I wouldn't invest in VOO, expecting these returns to continue over the long term (the index has gotten an AI-fueled boost in the past few years), but even if it averaged 10% annually, you could double your investment in 7.2 years. Ideally, though, you'd continue to occasionally add to your initial $1,000 investment.

The stock market tends to reward patience and consistency. VOO isn't a get-rich-quick investment or the next Nvidia or similar generational growth stock. However, it's as straightforward and dependable as you could want from a first-time investment.

If the U.S. economy continues to grow over time -- which it likely will -- so will VOO. You won't regret the investment.

When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 794%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 203% for the S&P 500.
