Fed's Waller Calls for 50-Basis-Point Rate Hike, Says Tokenized Finance Still Needs Traditional Banks

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Fed's Waller Calls for 50-Basis-Point Rate Hike, Says Tokenized Finance Still Needs Traditional Banks

Federal Reserve Governor Christopher Waller wants the central bank to get more aggressive with interest rate hikes, arguing that stubborn inflation may require a 50-basis-point increase in December rather than the smaller move investors expect.

Speaking during a CNBC interview Wednesday, Waller said the Fed must either deliver a half-percentage-point increase at its December 8-9 meeting or explicitly signal that another hike will follow in January.

“You might need to, like, shock people,” Waller said, referring to the central bank's efforts to prevent businesses and consumers from accepting inflation near 3% as normal.

“My view is then you have to go 50 in December to put it in place and get it done,” he said, warning that insufficiently aggressive action could weaken expectations that inflation will return to the Fed's target.

Waller's concerns follow three consecutive months in which core consumer prices rose 0.3%, a pace equivalent to roughly 3.5% inflation annually. That's considerably higher than the Fed's 2% target, which is formally measured using a separate inflation indicator known as the Personal Consumption Expenditures price index.

The governor had already established his threshold for a more aggressive move in a November 10 speech. He said another 0.3% core CPI reading would warrant considering a half-point hike, arguing that moving only 25 basis points could damage the Fed's credibility.

October inflation subsequently met that condition. Headline consumer prices rose 0.4% during the month, while core inflation climbed 0.3%, both exceeding economists' forecasts.

Waller's latest comments suggest he is increasingly concerned about the psychology of inflation, particularly the risk that companies begin treating annual price increases of 3% as acceptable and routinely pass higher costs to customers.

The proposed increase would lift the Fed's benchmark rate from its current range of 3.75%-4% to 4.25%-4.50%. Two consecutive quarter-point hikes in December and January would reach the same destination, but Waller argues that timing and communication matter when inflation expectations are at risk.

Markets remain less convinced that the Fed will move that aggressively in December. Futures pricing Wednesday morning showed roughly a 95% probability of a 25-basis-point increase and only about a 5% probability of a half-point move

The prospect of higher borrowing costs has been particularly painful for crypto investors. Higher interest rates make Treasury securities and other interest-bearing investments more competitive with speculative assets, while tighter financial conditions can reduce the money available for riskier bets.

Bitcoin has already suffered a sharp selloff amid the changing rate outlook. As Decrypt previously covered, persistent inflation fears have coincided with withdrawals from spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds and weakening technical indicators, adding pressure to an already fragile market.

Waller's increasingly hawkish monetary-policy stance comes alongside a more accommodating view of blockchain networks, although he sees established financial infrastructure as essential to its adoption.

In a separate November 17 speech at The Clearing House Annual Conference in New York, Waller discussed how tokenization—the representation of financial assets on digital networks—could reshape payments and securities markets. That speech focused on financial technology rather than interest-rate policy.

He pointed to a growing convergence between traditional markets and blockchain platforms, with banks and payment providers experimenting with tokenized deposits, stablecoins, and programmable transactions.

Waller described how tokenized money could allow investors to purchase securities outside conventional trading hours, with payments and assets changing hands simultaneously through smart contracts.

But he rejected the idea that the development of blockchain-based markets necessarily makes existing payment infrastructure obsolete.

“Central bank money need not be used in every transaction or on every platform, but its presence at the foundation of the system is essential,” Waller said.

Warner Bros. TV & Paramount TV Studios To Merge With Channing Dungey & Matt Thunell As Presidents

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Just like Paramount Television Studios’ first iteration, PTVS 2.0’s run as a standalone label was short-lived.

A month after the completion of Paramount and Warner Bros. Discovery’s merger into Skydance, two of the combined company’s three television studios, Warner Bros. Television Group and Paramount Television Studios, are being consolidated into one. The merger, planned for early 2027, comes 15 months after FTVS was resurrected following Skydance’s acquisition of Paramount.

The new entity, Warner Bros. Television Studios, will be co-run by WBTV’s Channing Dungey and PTVS’ Matt Thunell who have been named Presidents. They will continue to report to George Cheeks, Co-Chair and Chief Content Officer of Skydance TV. As previously announced, Dungey also serves as Chair of Skydance Global Cable Media, which combines the cable network portfolios of Warner Bros. Discovery and Paramount, featuring more than 50 brands. Related Stories Executives Skydance Cable Shakeup: Betsy Sanner Ayala, Jason Sarlanis, Tina Perry & Michael Ouweleen To Oversee Programming; Howard Lee Exiting Executives Louis Carr Leaving As BET President Amid Post-Merger Skydance TV Restructuring

There is no mention of layoffs in the studio restructuring announcement though cuts are inevitable when divisions are merged; it was also the case when PTVS 1.0 was folded into Skydance’s other TV studio, CBS Studios, in 2024, a move also overseen by Cheeks at the time. In his memo announcing the new combined studio, which you can read below, Cheeks alludes to the pending cuts, noting that “HR will share more information as plans are finalized.” Watch on Deadline

In smaller changes, BET Studios, which a year ago was moved to CBS Studios, is on the move again, this time relocating to Warner Bros. Television Studios. And Skydance Sports Entertainment, which was announced by Cheeks as part of the Skydance TV Studios Group last month “with details to follow,” will be housed at CBS Studios, which is led by President David Stapf.

Merging WBTV and PTVS is not surprising — putting WBTV, CBS Studios and PTVS in one one Skydance TV Studios unit under Cheeks positioned all three for future consolidation. The manner in which it happened is.

Skydance is doing the consolidation on similar time table as Disney, which folded Touchstone TV into 20th Television pretty quickly after the acquisition of Fox assets. (It took four years for the final step, merging the remaining two TV studios, 20th Television and ABC Signature.)

As Deadline previously noted, of the three Skydance TV studios, CBS Studios has the most clearly defined lane as the primary supplier to CBS (as well as home of Star Trek and an opportunistic third-party seller).

Meanwhile, there was significant overlap between Warner Bros Television and PTVS. They each had been tasked with mining the IP of their sister film studios and have been main suppliers to their sibling streamers HBO Max and Paramount+, respectively. Their common playing area is the open streaming market, as both are actively selling outside and have hits on multiple platforms.

With the Paramount and Warner Bros. movie studios under joint leadership and HBO Max and Paramount+ bound to merge into one, combining the two TV studios appeared inevitable — and was rumored even before the Paramount-WBD transaction was completed.

Making Dungey and Thunell equal co-leaders of the combined studio is raising eyebrows given that they are at different points of their career, with Dungey coming into the partnership with a lot more experience and a stature as one of the top, widely respected television executives in town.

Meanwhile, the well-liked Thunell was a rising star at David Ellison’s Skydance where he was President of TV, and his upward trajectory has continued at Paramount Skydance and now at post-WBD merger Skydance.

Given their different backgrounds, Thunell reporting to Dungey would not have been farfetched; that scenario was shut down when Cheeks announced Skydance TV Studios, with Channing, Thunell and Stapf all reporting to him.

Thunell taking over WBTV also had been rumored though not feasible right away due to the massive size of that studio’s operation and slate that dwarf PTVS. That may still be endgame as it’s unclear how long the current co-head structure will be in place. It would give Thunell time to get to know WBTV, working alongside studio veteran Dungey.

While Dungey is taking on additional responsibilities by adding the Paramount cable brands to her WBD cable portfolio, her sharing the reins of WBTV and reporting to Cheeks instead of Skydance’s CEO as was the case at WBD is noteworthy as the Hollywood glass ceiling has been increasingly coming into play over the past year or so with some high-profile departures of female executives, most recently Paramount’s Cindy Holland and Netflix’s Bela Bajaria, and the Disney CEO job going to Josh D’Amaro and not Dana Walden.

The new Warner Bros. Television Studios will be home to more than 120 active television series, including Harry Potter and The Philosopher’s Stone (HBO), The Pitt (HBO Max), Ted Lasso (Apple TV), Georgie & Mandy’s First Marriage (CBS), Reacher (Prime Video), Marshals (CBS), Landman (Paramount+), Emily in Paris (Netflix) and the upcoming Neuromancer (Apple TV).

“Bringing together two of the industry’s most prolific television studios creates a formidable creative engine, uniting extraordinary talent, resources, acclaimed programming and iconic franchises with the global scale to compete at the highest level. With Channing and Matt at the helm, we have two exceptional leaders who understand what it takes to empower creative talent, foster bold ideas and deliver programming that resonates with audiences everywhere,” Cheeks said. “Alongside CBS Studios, led by the incomparable David Stapf, Warner Bros. Television Studios gives us two world-class studios, with the breadth, depth and creative ambition to deliver for Skydance platforms and third-party partners across the industry.”

Said Dungey: “Matt has extraordinary creative instincts, and a deep understanding of great storytelling. I couldn’t ask for a better partner as we bring these storied studios together. We have a unique opportunity to create something truly special for our creative partners and audiences around the world. I’m very excited to begin this new chapter.”

Said Thunell: “Channing is one of the most accomplished and respected executives in our industry, with an extraordinary track record of building successful businesses, developing enduring partnerships and delivering some of television’s most acclaimed programming. Ever since our time together at Netflix, I’ve hoped we’d have the opportunity to work together again, and I’m thrilled that day has finally come. We’re bringing an unmatched roster of talent under one roof, building on the remarkable legacies of two storied studios. The possibilities ahead are extraordinary, and I couldn’t be more excited to partner with Channing to champion the ambitions of our creative talent and bring exceptional stories to audiences around the world.”

Darren Aronofsky’s Protozoa and Memory to Adapt Chris Miller’s ‘Chip War’ into Feature Documentary (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Chris Miller’s book “Chip War: The Fight for the World’s Most Critical Technology” is being adapted into a feature documentary from Darren Aronofsky‘s Protozoa and Memory, Variety can reveal exclusively. “All Light, Everywhere” filmmaker Theo Anthony will direct the film.

Producers include Riel Roch-Decter and Anthony for Memory, and Aronofsky, Dylan Golden and Brendan Naylor for Protozoa. Executive producers are Jessica Harrop for Sandbox Films, Jenny Raskin for Impact Partners and Rachel Crane for Cranium Productions. CAA Media Finance, which arranged financing for the film, represents its worldwide distribution rights. Related Stories ‘Musk’ Doc Gets International Release Dates Skydance Sports Chief Gives Top Roles to Jeff Gerttula, Craig Barry

According to an official description, the “Chip War” doc will explore “the semiconductor industry, the vast and largely invisible supply chain that makes the chips inside every phone, weapon and AI model, along with the escalating struggle between the United States and China to control it. Amid political upheaval, corporate scandal and a wave of upcoming AI IPOs, the race for chips has never been more urgent.”

“The story of silicon and the people who shaped and are shaping it is the story of our time. When I read Chris’ brilliant book, I knew it had to find more audiences with moving images. Who better to bring it to life than the absolute original filmmaking of Theo Anthony?” Aronofsky said.

Anthony made his feature debut in 2016 with “Rat Film,” an experimental doc about the rat infestation in Baltimore, which helps tell a bigger story about classism and racism in the city. He followed that project with his 2019 “30 for 30” film “Subject to Review,” chronicling the rise of the instant replay system in professional tennis. His last documentary, “All Light, Everywhere,” premiered at Sundance 2021 and won the special jury prize for nonfiction experimentation.

Anthony, Memory and Protozoa are represented by CAA.

Skydance to Fold Paramount TV Studios Into Warner Bros. TV Under Channing Dungey and Matt Thunell

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

CBS Studios will remain standalone production unit under David Stapf

Skydance is officially combining Warner Bros. Television Group and Paramount Television Studios, two of its key TV production units, Variety has learned.

The new combined entity will be known as Warner Bros. Television Studios, with Channing Dungey and Matt Thunell serving as presidents. They will report into George Cheeks, co-chair and chief content officer of Skydance TV. Cheeks made the announcement Wednesday morning in an internal memo to staff, which can be read in full below.

Dungey and Thunell will also oversee BET Studios and Warner Bros. International Television Production, while it was previously announced that Dungey also now serves as chair of Skydance Global Cable Media, which combines the cable network portfolios of Warner Bros. Discovery and Paramount.

The merger of the two studios will be completed in early 2027.

Meanwhile, CBS Studios will continue to operate as a standalone entity under David Stapf, also reporting to Cheeks. CBS Studios International, Skydance Sports Entertainment and See It Now Studios will operate under the CBS Studios umbrella.

“Bringing together two of the industry’s most prolific television studios creates a formidable creative engine, uniting extraordinary talent, resources, acclaimed programming and iconic franchises with the global scale to compete at the highest level,” Cheeks said. “With Channing and Matt at the helm, we have two exceptional leaders who understand what it takes to empower creative talent, foster bold ideas and deliver programming that resonates with audiences everywhere. Alongside CBS Studios, led by the incomparable David Stapf, Warner Bros. Television Studios gives us two world-class studios, with the breadth, depth and creative ambition to deliver for Skydance platforms and third-party partners across the industry.”

It had previously been speculated that PTVS would be merged back into CBS Studios as it had been before. But the move to combine PTVS into one of the other studio units is not surprising. WBTV is a major powerhouse, with its current slate across a number of networks and streamers including shows like “The Pitt,” “Ted Lasso,” “Shrinking,” “Georgie & Mandy’s First Marriage,” “Abbott Elementary,” and the upcoming “Harry Potter” HBO series. “Matt has extraordinary creative instincts, and a deep understanding of great storytelling,” Dungey said. “I couldn’t ask for a better partner as we bring these storied studios together. “We have a unique opportunity to create something truly special for our creative partners and audiences around the world. I’m very excited to begin this new chapter.”

Paramount Television Studios currently produces or co-produces shows such as “Reacher” at Prime Video and Taylor Sheridan’s Paramount+ shows, including “Landman,” “Tulsa King,” “Dutton Ranch” and more. However, PTVS has been shut down and revived multiple times under various names, with Skydance only bringing the division back in its current form in 2025 upon the completion of the Skydance-Paramount merger.

“Channing is one of the most accomplished and respected executives in our industry, with an extraordinary track record of building successful businesses, developing enduring partnerships and delivering some of television’s most acclaimed programming,” Thunell said. “Ever since our time together at Netflix, I’ve hoped we’d have the opportunity to work together again, and I’m thrilled that day has finally come. We’re bringing an unmatched roster of talent under one roof, building on the remarkable legacies of two storied studios. The possibilities ahead are extraordinary, and I couldn’t be more excited to partner with Channing to champion the ambitions of our creative talent and bring exceptional stories to audiences around the world.”

CBS Studios, meanwhile, continues to produce a long string of hit shows under Stapf and his team. The studio still has the CBS cornerstone “NCIS” on the air in its 24th season, with the franchise currently encompassing the prequel “NCIS: Origins” and “NCIS: New York.” CBS Studios also co-produces “Criminal Minds,” which aired for 15 seasons on CBS and was revived for an additional five at Paramount+.

The studio also has shows like the “FBI” franchise, the hit comedy “Ghosts,” “Elsbeth,” the “Fire Country” franchise, and “Little House on the Prairie” at Netflix. Meanwhile, consistent hitmakers Robert and Michelle King, who have called CBS Studios home for more than two decades, just launched to legal drama “Cupertino” on CBS via CBS Studios.

This marks the latest moves under the combined Skydance-Warner Bros. Discovery under David Ellision. In addition to the previously announced cable networks reorganization, it was reported earlier this week that Skydance Motion Picture Group co-chairs Dana Goldberg and Josh Greenstein have set their leadership team.

Co-Chair & Chief Content Officer, Skydance TV

Today, I’m sharing an important next step for how our television studios will serve the creative community. We are combining the Warner Bros. Television Group and Paramount Television Studios into one studio, which will operate as Warner Bros. Television Studios, beginning in early 2027. Until then, both studios will continue to operate independently.

This move aligns our leadership, resources and creative focus for two studios with considerable success producing for streaming, as well as broadcast. By bringing these teams together, we can build on their strengths and create an even stronger home for great talent and great storytelling.

Channing Dungey and Matt Thunell will be Presidents of Warner Bros. Television Studios, jointly leading the combined studio and reporting to me, with oversight of its creative departments and an expansive and growing portfolio of scripted and unscripted programming. Home to many of the Network’s biggest franchises and hits, David and his team remain a critical supplier to our broadcast business while continuing to grow their streaming footprint. CBS Studios International, Skydance Sports Entertainment and See It Now Studios will operate under the CBS Studios umbrella.

Collectively, Warner Bros. Television Studios and CBS Studios present two powerful creative engines with extraordinary talent, award-winning programming, hit franchises and the global scale to serve Skydance platforms and third-party partners across the industry. Add to that, CBS Entertainment, led by Amy Reisenbach, and the Global Cable Media team under Channing, Skydance TV is a creative force in entertainment across scripted, unscripted and live events.

Channing, Matt and David will be connecting with teams in the coming weeks to talk more about the new structure. We know there will be questions about what this means for individual teams. HR will share more information as plans are finalized.

I’m excited about what we’re building. We have extraordinary creative talent, an unmatched portfolio and tremendous opportunity ahead. Bringing these teams together gives us an even stronger foundation to support storytellers, champion great ideas and bring exceptional television to audiences around the world.

Warner Bros. TV, Paramount TV Studios to Combine as a Single Mega-Studio

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Channing Dungey and Matt Thunell will lead the combined studio, while CBS Studios remains separate under David Stapf.

Skydance is making a big move with its TV studios, combining Warner Bros. Television and Paramount Television Studios into a single entity that will have more than 100 productions under its umbrella.

The combined studio will be known as Warner Bros. Television Studios, a nod to the larger of the two current units. The new organization will officially launch early next year, with each group operating as usual until then. WBTV chief Channing Dungey and PTVS head Matt Thunell will be presidents of the combined studio, reporting to Skydance TV co-chair and chief content officer George Cheeks. Dungey also continues to lead Skydance’s cable portfolio.

CBS Studios, headed by David Stapf, will keep operating as its own entity. CBS Studios International, Skydance Sports Entertainment and nonfiction unit See It Now Studios fall under CBS Studios. BET Studios will become part of Warner Bros. TV Studios.

“Bringing together two of the industry’s most prolific television studios creates a formidable creative engine, uniting extraordinary talent, resources, acclaimed programming and iconic franchises with the global scale to compete at the highest level,” Cheeks said in a statement. “With Channing and Matt at the helm, we have two exceptional leaders who understand what it takes to empower creative talent, foster bold ideas and deliver programming that resonates with audiences everywhere. Alongside CBS Studios, led by the incomparable David Stapf, Warner Bros. Television Studios gives us two world-class studios, with the breadth, depth and creative ambition to deliver for Skydance platforms and third-party partners across the industry.”

Dungey and Thunell — who previously worked together at Netflix — offered praise for their soon-to-be partnership in statements of their own.

“Matt has extraordinary creative instincts, and a deep understanding of great storytelling. I couldn’t ask for a better partner as we bring these storied studios together,” said Dungey. “We have a unique opportunity to create something truly special for our creative partners and audiences around the world. I’m very excited to begin this new chapter.”

Added Thunell, “Channing is one of the most accomplished and respected executives in our industry, with an extraordinary track record of building successful businesses, developing enduring partnerships and delivering some of television’s most acclaimed programming. Ever since our time together at Netflix, I’ve hoped we’d have the opportunity to work together again, and I’m thrilled that day has finally come.”

Skydance combining the two TV studios follows a similar playbook to other big media mergers in the past decade. After Disney took over 21st Century Fox’s entertainment assets, four TV studios eventually became three, then two and now just one (20th Television). A year before its own 2025 merger with Skydance, Paramount shut down Paramount TV Studios, then revived the name under as it combined Skydance TV and Showtime/MTV Entertainment under Thunell.

It’s also worth noting that the antitrust lawsuit settlement that paved the way for the Paramount-Warner Bros. Discovery merger did not explicitly mention TV studio operations, even as it set benchmarks for the number of feature film releases from the Warner Bros. and Paramount movie studios. The primary TV provisions in the settlement concern carriage agreements with cable and satellite operators and the editorial independence of CBS News and CNN. Even though the combined Warner Bros. TV Studios and CBS Studios produce some 150 series, including international co-productions, that side of the business went unremarked on.

The combination of the two studios will also likely result in layoffs. “We know there will be questions about what this means for individual teams,” Cheeks wrote in a memo to staff Wednesday (read it in full, below). “HR will share more information as plans are finalized.” It will also mean one fewer place for writers and producers to pitch and sell series projects.

The text of Cheeks’ memo outlining the changes is below.

Team,Today, I’m sharing an important next step for how our television studios will serve the creative community. We are combining the Warner Bros. Television Group and Paramount Television Studios into one studio, which will operate as Warner Bros. Television Studios, beginning in early 2027. Until then, both studios will continue to operate independently.This move aligns our leadership, resources and creative focus for two studios with considerable success producing for streaming, as well as broadcast. By bringing these teams together, we can build on their strengths and create an even stronger home for great talent and great storytelling.Channing Dungey and Matt Thunell will be Presidents of Warner Bros. Television Studios, jointly leading the combined studio and reporting to me, with oversight of its creative departments and an expansive and growing portfolio of scripted and unscripted programming. They are exceptional creative leaders who understand how to support talent, champion bold ideas and build strong partnerships across our industry. Channing and Matt will also oversee BET Studios and Warner Bros. International Television Production studios around the world.David Stapf continues in his role as President of CBS Studios, reporting to me, building on the studio’s strength as one of the industry’s leading global studios. Home to many of the Network’s biggest franchises and hits, David and his team remain a critical supplier to our broadcast business while continuing to grow their streaming footprint. CBS Studios International, Skydance Sports Entertainment and See It Now Studios will operate under the CBS Studios umbrella.Collectively, Warner Bros. Television Studios and CBS Studios present two powerful creative engines with extraordinary talent, award-winning programming, hit franchises and the global scale to serve Skydance platforms and third-party partners across the industry. Add to that, CBS Entertainment, led by Amy Reisenbach, and the Global Cable Media team under Channing, Skydance TV is a creative force in entertainment across scripted, unscripted and live events.What Happens NextChanning, Matt and David will be connecting with teams in the coming weeks to talk more about the new structure. Video: Need A Break Between GTA Reviews? Watch Us Rave About Zelda's Switch 2 Changes

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Now yes, things have been a little Vice City-focused today, haven't they? The GTA 6 reviews are out, and it is, by all accounts, bloody brilliant.

But you know what else is brilliant? The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time.

While most of the internet is off Leonida dreamin' today (and honestly, fair enough), we've still got Hyrule on the mind. Shuckin' Shack Communities Rally to Turn the Tide Against Blood Cancer With a Record Breaking $123,805.16 Raised

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The 18-unit seafood franchise has capped off its fifth year of events supporting Blood Cancer United with record-breaking numbers, Franchise Partner-led initiatives and a deep community impact.

WILMINGTON, N.C., Nov. 18, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Following a month-long blitz dedicated to supporting local patients and blood cancer research, Shuckin' Shack Oyster Bar, the 18-unit seafood restaurant franchise, has wrapped up its fifth annual campaign supporting Blood Cancer United. The campaign spanned the entire month of September, Blood Cancer Awareness Month, with Franchise Partners driving tailor-made initiatives and offers at local Shacks as part of a shared, systemwide goal to Turn the Tide against blood cancer.

This year's campaign was the most successful one yet, raising a record breaking $123,805 through in-store events, online donations, merchandise sales and community outreach. The month-long effort culminated in a Sept. 26 One Day, One Cause finale, where Shacks throughout the system hosted events to cap off the month and celebrate the change made by the Franchise Partners, team members and guests who rallied around the cause.

"Giving each location the ability to do their own thing â create offers and events that they knew their communities and guests would love â resulted in great buy-in throughout the system," said Co-Founder Matt Piccinin. "Sept. 26 was a great day for One Day, One Cause. The Carolina Beach location, for example, had its best one-day event in five years, bringing in $44,000 on that Saturday alone."

The record-breaking campaign puts the 5 year annual total over $400,000 raised, all thanks to local Shack communities who stepped up in the fight against blood cancer. Systemwide, Franchise Partners brought their own creative energy to the month, hosting everything from beach parties to "paint and sip" nights where guests decorated holiday oyster shells. In Frederick, Maryland, Franchise Partner Eric Weller, whose mother passed away from leukemia, united his staff and community around the cause and drove nearly $22,000 in digital donations. Throughout the month, people shared their stories and memories of loved ones on Shuckin' Shack's socials and in the Shacks they visited.

"For one month, in our Shack communities, everyone is getting together for one cause," said Darren Keeler, Vice President of Marketing and Creative. "During One Day, One Cause, we saw a lot of repeat visitors, including people who are currently going through treatment. It's really positive to see them show up consistently for the past five years. Their family members will fly in for these events; their groups just keep growing and growing."

Family members of patients who had recently passed away and previously enjoyed attending Shuckin' Shack's benefit events were also in attendance. Keeler explained that, for one family, it was especially emotional as it was their first year without their loved one at the event. "It was heartwarming because they know that Blood Cancer United's research added days to her life, and this was another way for them to honor and celebrate her," he said.

"When we talk about community, this is what it's about," said CEO Jonathan Weathington. "We have our own ties with Blood Cancer United through Matt, Eric's mom and several others in our system. Cancer touches everyone. Turn The Tide is what actual community looks like; it's not a scenario where we're forcing people's hands and making a big scene. There may be some publicity that comes from it, but the heart of it is altruistic."

Shuckin' Shack will continue to build on this record momentum. With a Turn the Tide campaign already scheduled for next year, the brand remains committed to deepening its community roots, empowering its Franchise Partners and raising the bar each September in the fight against blood cancer.

Offering a "lifestyle experience," Shuckin' Shack Oyster Bar creates an environment that encourages guests to grab a seat, relax and take their taste buds on a trip to the coast; after all, life is too short for bland drinks and boring bars! The original Carolina Beach, NC Shack, which was just 900 square feet, was launched in 2007 with a plan to become the go-to local hangout for people of all ages. A place where friends and family can enjoy fresh, delicious meals, cold beer and craft cocktails in an environment that exudes fun and relaxation, Shuckin' Shack offers exceptional seafood and creative drinks that will make guests question why they ever settled for ordinary bar offerings.

Thanks to its bold flavors and impeccable vibes, Shuckin' Shack has continued to grow. In 2014, the brand began franchising and has since expanded to 18 units across six states. To learn more about Shuckin' Shack, visit http://www.theshuckinshack.com.

16 Ecuadorian fishermen sue US government, allege drone attack destroyed their boat near the Galápagos

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A Sunday strike against a low-profile vessel that intelligence showed was participating in "narco-trafficking," killing two people on board, according to U.S. Southern Command. (Credit: U.S. SOUTHCOM)

More than a dozen Ecuadorian fishermen have sued the federal government, alleging they were wrongly targeted in a drone strike that destroyed their boat in the Pacific Ocean earlier this year as part of the Trump administration’s broader campaign against vessels suspected of drug trafficking.

The 16 fishermen aboard La Negra Francisca Duarte II say drones launched from a U.S. vessel struck their boat March 17, 2026, as they headed home from a two-week fishing trip near the Galápagos.

The strike set the boat ablaze, forcing the crew to jump overboard and escape in lifeboats, according to a lawsuit filed Monday in federal court in Pennsylvania. All 16 survived, but two suffered serious burns. One man’s leg was burned to the bone, the lawsuit says.

The fishermen’s lawyers say the crew had no ties to drug cartels or terrorist groups and posed no imminent threat when U.S. drones struck their vessel, which they say had been cleared by Ecuadorian authorities hours earlier.

This still image from Sept. 19, 2026, shows the moment before a projectile hits a vessel in the Caribbean the U.S. military alleged was involved in drug trafficking. (US Southern Command/Handout via Reuters)

US MILITARY LAUNCHES FIRST AUTONOMOUS WARFARE COMMAND TO DEPLOY UNMANNED SYSTEMS ACROSS LATIN AMERICA

After their boat was destroyed, the fishermen sailed toward a blue vessel, which the lawsuit identified as a U.S. ship. Some personnel wore U.S. Coast Guard vests, the suit says. They allegedly zip-tied the uninjured men, hooded them and forced them to stand barefoot on the hot metal deck with no food and little water.

One injured fisherman was taken below deck for treatment, where U.S. personnel allegedly told him they had carried out the drone strike and laughed about destroying the boat, according to the lawsuit.

The fishermen were held until the next day before they were put on an El Salvadoran ship that took them back to El Salvador, where they were held at a migrant center in "deplorable conditions" until the end of March.

Demonstrators gather to protest against the deportation of immigrants to El Salvador outside the Permanent Mission of El Salvador to the United Nations on April 24, 2025, in New York City. (Michael M. Santiago/Getty Images)

THE UNLIKELY SOUTH AMERICAN ALLY HELPING LEAD TRUMP'S DRUG CARTEL CRACKDOWN

The Department of War and the U.S. Coast Guard have denied knowing about or being involved in the March 17 attack on La Negra Francisca Duarte II.

U.S. Southern Command declined to comment, citing its policy of not issuing statements on pending or ongoing litigation.

A suspected drug-trafficking vessel erupts in flames following a U.S. military strike in the Eastern Pacific on June 18, 2026. (U.S. Southern Command)

The lawsuit argues that the Trump administration’s strikes in the Caribbean and Pacific violate federal and international law because they have been carried out outside an armed conflict. It adds that even if an armed conflict existed, the attacks could constitute war crimes under the Geneva Conventions.

The plaintiffs are seeking compensatory and other financial damages to be determined at trial, plus an injunction ordering the U.S. to end similar boat attacks.

James Cirrone is a writer on the Breaking/Trending News team at Fox News Digital. Story tips can be sent to james.cirrone@fox.com.

ZIM (ZIM) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Premium expedited services and high-value cargo drove a sharp profit jump

By Motley Fool Transcribing – Nov 18, 2026 at 1:11PM EST

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

ZIM (ZIM +1.21%) reported quarterly financial results and raised full-year performance guidance for the second time in 2026. Management reported a strategic focus on profitable trade lanes between Asia and North America, specifically expanding premium expedited services to the U.S. West Coast. The company reported a decline in vessel operating costs driven by fleet modernization and the introduction of larger, more fuel-efficient ships. Management stated that the current network strategy prioritizes high-value cargo categories, including e-commerce, AI data center components, and pharmaceuticals, while capping volumes assigned to standard annual contracts. The company confirmed it is continuing to engage with regulatory authorities regarding its pending merger with Hapag-Lloyd, which is currently targeted for completion in the first quarter of 2027.

Operator: Hello, everyone. Thank you for joining us, and welcome to the ZIM Integrated Shipping Services Third Quarter 2026 Financial Results Conference Call. [Operator Instructions] I will now hand the conference over to Elana Holzman, Head of Investor Relations. Elana, please go ahead.

Elana Holzman: Thank you, operator, and welcome to ZIM's Third Quarter 2026 Financial Results Conference Call. Joining me on the call today are Chen Lichtenstein, ZIM's President and CEO; and Sami Jubran, ZIM's CFO. Before we begin, I would like to remind you that during the course of this call, we will make forward-looking statements regarding expectations, predictions, projections or future events or results. We believe that our expectations and assumptions are reasonable. We wish to caution you that such statements reflect only the company's current expectations and that actual events or results may differ, including materially.

You are kindly referred to consider the risk factors and cautionary language described in the documents the company filed with the Securities and Exchange Commission, including our 2025 annual report on Form 20-F filed with the SEC on March 9, 2026. We undertake no obligation to update these forward-looking statements. We would also like to remind you that during this call, we will not be able to address any questions relating to our pending merger transaction with Hapag-Lloyd. At this time, I would like to turn the call over to ZIM's CEO, Chen Lichtenstein. Chen?

Since joining ZIM, my priority has been very clear: to capitalize fully on the current market opportunities by proactive allocation of the company's resources. We optimize every voyage, we maximize profitability, and we give exceptional attention to every detail to maximize also return to our shareholders. Today, we will discuss the third quarter performance, current market conditions, and we will provide an update on the outlook for the remainder of the year. We're really pleased to report industry-leading profitability, both in quarter 3 and the year-to-date.

The results demonstrate the strength of the differentiated business model of ZIM, leading position in key trades, especially the Transpacific, and the ability to respond quickly to changing market conditions. If we move on to Page 3 in the presentation, I'll just touch on a few of the points on this page. So revenues of $2.7 billion, 53% higher than the prior year quarter. In fact, with the guidance, the updated guidance that we will be providing, one can calculate the net leverage ratio further reduction to a very low number by the end of the year. We're further increasing our guidance, which we'll touch on in a second. We've been capitalizing on our most profitable trades. So we launched or are in the process of launching the ZX3, which is our third premium expedited service from Asia to the West Coast of the United States.

And we've launched the Falcon, which is providing faster transit time from China to Argentina and Southern Brazil. We also continue to secure additional newbuild capacity at attractive costs, and we have today a total pipeline of 60% of our existing fleet capacity. Long-term attractive LNG supply agreement is a key part of our cost trajectory, which is going down. We have today the largest proportion of the fleet, which is LNG-powered, and we're very happy with the supply agreement, long-term supply agreement, which is in place. This is market, not us. We can see that the trajectory of 2026, which is clearly different from the trajectory that we've seen in prior years. And it, of course, connects to the congestion in the ports, especially in China, to the situation earlier in the year in the Panama Canal and obviously, the Hormuz and Bab-el-Mandeb congestion. So what we see, if we look into West Coast -- U.S. West Coast -- Asia to U.S. West Coast and Asia to U.S. East Coast trajectories, we see also very high conditions, very strong conditions. And unlike in prior years when there was a reduction at the end of the summer, we don't see that this year.

We do see some softening in recent weeks. We think it's related to people prolonging their orders before Christmas and the end of year, so they don't want to get products into the U.S. during this time. And there is an expectation that the market will pick up again in January before the Chinese New Year, which is a bit earlier this year. Moving on to Page 5. This is the page where we discuss our revised guidance. We provided guidance in August, revised it once before, and now it's a second revision upwards.

So we have a guidance for the end of year 2026 of EBITDA of USD 3.3 billion to USD 3.5 billion for adjusted EBITDA and adjusted EBIT of $2 billion to $2.2 billion. This implies, of course, if you take the adjusted EBITDA or EBIT and deduct what we have already delivered by the end of Q3, it gives a midpoint projection of EBITDA for Q4 of $1.27 billion and midpoint projection for adjusted EBIT in Q4 of $943 million, which is 70x what we delivered in the same period of Q4 last year and 4x the EBITDA that was delivered in Q4 of last year. If we move on to the next page, Page 6.

This is the dividend calculation. So we're declaring $2.01 per share. This is calculated as a total of $243 million, which is 30% of the 9 months 2026 aggregate net income. This dividend is declared according to our policy, which is consistently applied over the years. If we look into Page 7, a quick update on the pending transaction with Hapag-Lloyd. Of course, subject to all the regulatory approvals and to the approval of the State of Israel regarding its Golden Share. If I look into our focused and agile approach, which is delivering this industry-leading profitability. We can point to a few items, which I observed in my -- since I joined the company. So first is a massive concentration on the profitable trades connecting Asia and the world.

The ability of the company to really optimize the cargo mix and freight rates vessel by vessel, journey by journey as we move along, expanding the premium and expedited services across the Pacific. And in terms of the fleet strategy, today we control a very modern fleet, whereas 86% of our vessels are either owned or under long-term charter contracts. We have a very cost-effective fleet with a long-term declining cost trajectory, which is coming both from the agreements that we have where many of the costs per year are declining. And also as we introduce more capacity, we have larger vessels, so the cost per TEU is coming down.

And we have, as I mentioned, the highest proportion of LNG capacity in our fleet with a very attractive LNG supply agreement. Moving on to Page 9. The focus on the Pacific, on the Transpacific and also on the connection between China and Southeast Asia and the Rest of the World is not a coincidence. It's basically coming from a very clear focus on these trades as the trades that carry the most significant and high-value growth opportunity. But more importantly, or not less importantly, is that the value of this growth has grown -- the value of this growth has been growing even faster, so 38% of growth in value. And obviously, we see today that what's being delivered from Asia to the Rest of the World is high technology, critical elements for supply chains, for data centers, e-commerce, pharmaceuticals that require, of course, refrigeration, clean energy, storage and so forth. So we not only see growth in volumes, but growth in value. Moving on to the next page. We can see how this connects to the main growth drivers of our premium services.

So e-commerce growth is feeding a lot of our expedited premium services across the Pacific. And we see that, that is projected to grow at 7.5%, way higher than the overall growth of cargoes to 2030. AI and data centers, we have a task force, and we're very focused on connecting with the suppliers of the AI and data centers. And we see that spend, of the relevant spend to be shipped from Asia to North America, with a growth of 15% -- more than 15% average growth rate into 2030. And also the expedited services are converting the freights from air to ocean, and this is also another growth area that we're very focused on.

The layout of the company in terms of trades shows 50% the Transpacific, Intra-Asia, which is the largest trade in the world, we have 10% of our business there. So if we put the 2 together, we're talking about 60% of Intra-Asia and Transpacific. And then the most critical element in the rest of our network are the ones that cross from Asia to the Suez and Mediterranean to the Atlantic to Latin America and also our PCC business is mostly carrying cars from Asia to the Rest of the World. So as we see ourselves as #10 in the industry, once we move into the Transpacific trades, we're #7. The Asia to U.S.

East Coast, we're already #4 and Asia to the U.S. West Coast with the expedited services, we are already today #1. So we're focusing a lot of the effort and the energy and the capital of the company into further growing in those spaces where we have plenty of room to grow. If we move on to Page 12, we see that the ZEX line that was the first in the family going from Southern China, from Shenzhen, from Yantian and these areas and also connecting into Vietnam and benefiting from our synchronized Intra-Asia business, which is fully aligned with our Transpacific business. So this was the ZEX service. The ZX2 service connected Shanghai and Ningbo with Los Angeles.

And as of December, we are launching ZX3, which will connect Xiamen and Shanghai with to Los Angeles. So we will have 3 services in operation, servicing 7 different ports along the Eastern Seaboard of China and Vietnam and we're offering a synchronized interconnectivity with the rest of Southeast Asia through our Intra-Asia network. Moving on to Page 13. This is just a photo of 2 of our vessels in Yantian port in Shenzhen. You can see that the Transpacific vessel, the larger one, berthing just next to the smaller one that comes from our Intra-Asia services.

So we have the ability to fully synchronize between the arrival of the Intra-Asia vessel and the departure of the Transpacific one, so we can offer really best-in-class services for our expedited customers. On the U.S. side, on Page 14, we can see the offloading of our vessel in Long Beach, Los Angeles, where we again offer interconnectivity. So customers can ask for peel piles or to have dedicated trucks waiting for them upon arrival. And the cargoes can be out of gate immediately as they get off the vessel. Moving on to Page 15. I've looked into our strategic commercial side. What's the approach to our customers and to the way we fill our vessels?

And what we see here on the right is that the ability to deploy and redeploy vessels continuously to the most profitable routes. We're maximizing payloads for each and every voyage. So we're basically trying to optimize the weight of the cargo, so that we can charge the highest rate per container and per voyage. We're optimizing the cargo mix. And of course, we're targeting premium and high-value cargoes and customers. All of this is done by a dedicated sales team, and we highlight here that we're proudly using humans to do all this work, which requires a lot of attention to each vessel, which is departing to maximize profitability.

We also actively manage the cargo intake and maximize the profitability by capping what's called annual contracts volume, which to my experience also as a customer of ZIM and other companies in the past -- shipping companies, these kind of long-term contracts are holding for a year, which means on average, it's 6 months if you have a situation of declining prices and provides very little benefit and does take away a lot of the ability to maximize the freight rates.

So what you see on the left-hand side is a very critical analysis that I've asked to see, and this is our freight rate, our average freight rate for each year of the last 5 against the band of the industry. So we can see the highest peer and lowest peer for each period. And we see that 4 out of 5 periods, ZIM has been well above the carriers of the top 10 that provide this -- disclose this information. We had 1-year, which was a correction after COVID, where freight rates declined sharply.

And then you can see that we were only able to get freight rates in the middle of the pack, but I think it's well worth it for what we're able to deliver in each one of the other periods, including the first 9 months of this year and of course, quarter 3 itself, where on both cases, we're way ahead of any number that was published by our larger peers. If we look into the next page, Page 16, we can see that freight forwarders are key to our commercial success. Why? Because the high-value cargoes, the premium cargoes that we are focusing on with the ZEX family of services, they are mostly handled by freight forwarders.

And what we can show here, we don't provide the details of the specific average freight rates to the forwarders versus the cargo owners, but we show the arrows that show clearly that in each one of the last 3 years, from Asia to U.S. East Coast and from Asia to U.S. West Coast, we did get higher freight rates paid to us on average for each year by the forwarders. So this proves to be a very successful approach, which allows us to focus on the high-value cargoes, the high-value premium services and to charge higher for that.

If we move on now to the next slide, 17, another piece of analysis that I've asked to see is our lower vessel costs, which are driven by, on the one hand, our fleet modernization. So as newer and larger and more efficient vessels are coming into service, and the declining contracted rate trajectory. So when we sign a contract to lease a vessel, sometimes the rate stays flat throughout the period of lease, which could be 5 or 10 or 12 years or in many cases, it's a declining scale, which is all set in advance.

So what we see here is that from 2022, if we -- which we kind of benchmark or index to 100, we can see a clear decline of 30% until the end of quarter 3 this year because of the combination of the new vessels that come into service and the reducing rate trajectories on our leased vessels. So this is a trend that we know exactly how it behaves. We can forecast it also going forward. So this is kind of the dotted line that we show on this page, which shows our confidence that with the time going by, our fleet will become more and more and more efficient, continue to go in the same direction.

So if we now put together the higher freight rates that we're able to charge in almost any market condition with the declining trajectory of cost, these 2 together drive what we see on Page 18, which is our industry-leading EBIT profit -- operating profit per TEU. What we see here is that, again, with the exception of 1 period, when we were below the pack. In 2022, we were quite close to the top of the pack. This is, again, the larger peers of ours, this is the range which is shown, larger peers of ours that disclose this information.

We can see that in 2024, we achieved EBIT per TEU of $680, which is $31 more than the closest highest peer. In 2025, we achieved $242, which is $11 above the highest peer. And now we're opening a gap of $64 in the first 9 months of 2026, so well, well above the highest of the peer group at $421 EBIT per TEU. So this, I think, is the highest ever gap that we had, and it shows how the combination of the very focused commercial approach together with the declining cost trajectory work together to put us in this very, very strong, both absolute and relative position.

So with that, I will close and hand over to Sami, please.

Sami Jubran: Thank you very much, Chen. Good morning to you all. It is a pleasure to speak with you today as ZIM, CFO. ZIM has been my professional home for nearly 2 decades. And I'm excited to work together with Chen and the talented teams to continue strengthening ZIM's leadership position. Turning to our third quarter financial results. We present key highlights on Slide 20 and 21. Our third quarter results were once again strong, both on sequential and year-over-year basis. Beginning on Slide 20, we can see that our Q3 results or carried volume increased by 4%, while our average freight rate per TEU increased by 55% year-over-year.

This drove third quarter revenue of $2.7 billion that was 53% higher compared to last year. In other words, our financial debt is approximately $70 million. Turning to Slide 21. We provide additional financial highlights.

Third quarter revenues were up by 53% compared to last year, and total revenues in the first 9 months of 2026 were $5.9 billion, an increase of $470 million or 9% year-over-year. We generated adjusted EBITDA of $1.3 billion for the quarter, a 124% decrease (sic) [ increase ] year-over-year, while our total adjusted EBITDA for the first 9 months of 2026 was $2.1 billion, a 16% increase compared to last year. Adjusted EBITDA margin for the third quarter and the first 9 months of 2026 was 49% and 36%, respectively. Our margin improvement is a direct result of our strategic decisions, and we continue to manage our network to carefully control costs and allocate resources.

This includes maximizing our revenues by focusing daily on our most profitable line rotations and cargo mix. These actions have resulted in ZIM achieving better margins than many of its peers. Our adjusted EBIT also saw a large increase year-over-year of 282% from $260 million to $993 million. Total adjusted EBIT in the first 9 months of 2026 was 33% higher than the first 9 months of 2025. Third quarter net income was $830 million, up by 573% year-over-year. And adjusted net income was $717 million, up by 478% year-over-year. Turning to Slide 22. We see that ZIM's average freight rate has trended significantly upwards during the first 3 quarters of 2026.

Our average freight rate per TEU in the third quarter was $2,480 per TEU compared to $1,602 per TEU in the third quarter of last year, reflecting a 55% increase. Q3 freight rates were also up by 56% compared to Q2 2026. Q3 carried volume of 962,000 TEUs was 4% higher year-over-year, mostly due to increased volumes in the Transpacific, Transatlantic and Intra-Asia. Consistent with what, Chen, had mentioned earlier, we saw Q3 growth in Intra-Asia of 12% and Transpacific growth of 8% year-over-year. Next, we move to our cash flow for the third quarter and the first 9 months of 2026. We generated $1.2 billion of free cash flow for the third quarter.

We have also generated $1.9 billion of free cash flow in the 9-month period of 2026, increasing our cash position by $561 million since the start of the year. Turning to the next 2 slides, I would like to spend a few moments discussing our overall vessel strategy and steps we are taking to keep our fleet modern and competitive. The most important points to highlight are the optionality we maintain moving forward and our fixed long-term vessel costs. Of the charter agreements we have today, 94% of the capacity are for long-term durations of above 3 years and 86% are 5 years or longer. Of these long-term fixed agreements, 55% of the capacity include purchase options.

This allows us to maintain a stable and de-escalating vessel cost going forward. We provide an overview of our fleet on Slide 26. ZIM currently operates 125 vessels, 112 containerships with a total capacity of 703,000 TEUs plus 13 car carriers. Of this capacity, 60% are newbuilds and 44% are LNG-powered. Fleet flexibility remains central to our strategy. We have the options to redeliver several additional vessels this year and another 14 in 2027, allowing us to adjust our operated capacity as needed. Following the fleet upgrade program completed in 2023 and 2024, we remain focused on maintaining a modern and cost-competitive fleet.

To advance this objective, we have entered into charter agreements for a total 58 vessels, 2 of which we received recently. The outstanding charter order of 56 vessels range in size from 3,000 to 12,000 TEUs each. The total capacity of these vessels is 418,000 TEUs that represent approximately 60% of our capacity today. These agreements include 18 LNG vessels of 11,500 TEU each scheduled for delivery between 2027 and 2029, 2 12,000 TEU vessels scheduled for delivery in 2027 and 2 carriers with scheduled delivery in 2029, for which we have also purchase options. Importantly, the versatile range of our newbuilds will enable us to continue to actively manage our profitability even on a voyage level to maximize earnings.

Will Wright's next simulation will use AI to mine content from your memories

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

With MyMems, Wright says he wants to “turn the simulation inward.”

The computer gaming world hasn’t heard much from Will Wright (SimCity, The Sims) since the underwhelming release of Spore in 2008 and Wright’s subsequent departure from Maxis owner EA. Now, well over 15 years later, Wright is starting to promote his next digital simulation, which will use generative AI technology to craft a game-like experience from players’ memories.

MyMems, which is slated to release next year, differentiates itself from Wright’s previous projects by “turn[ing] the simulation inward,” as Wright’s Gallium Studios and partner Artificial Agency put it in a press release this morning. The game will mine memories provided by the player to create digital people that can then form teams to solve puzzles in the game world.

“If my Grandmother ended up in a Grand Slam match against Serena Williams, who would win?” Wright says by way of example in the release. “If I had to build a bridge across a short chasm, which three people from my life would I choose to help me?”

In an accompanying announcement video, Wright talks about how the “plausible behavior” in a game like The Sims still didn’t effectively capture the nuances of people he encounters in real life. “It was nowhere near them understanding my claustrophobia or why my sister and I fought so much… We’re actually taking parts of your life, your memories, your experiences, people you know, and turning that into a game that’s very personal.”

Wright said MyMems will be able to extract generalized themes from your memories into self-contained areas that Wright compared to the various parts in a theme park: Sports, vacations, injuries, memories, and deaths could all be the basis for their own zones to play in. Players will also have the option to create worlds based on shared memories with family, friends, and co-workers, for example.

Since this is 2026, it shouldn’t be surprising that MyMems is making heavy use of generative AI (with a “proprietary behavior engine” on top) to transform your typed-up memories into simulated locales and characters. In the announcement video, Wright praises the “entirely open-ended” AI systems Gallium is using “to extract from the stories and whatnot based upon the memories you put into the game.”

But Wright’s focus on mining memories for simulation content predates the current generative AI boom. What has now become MyMems started off as Proxi, a mobile simulation that was first announced in 2018 as “a game of self-discovery, a game where we actually uncover the hidden you.” But after years of pre-production work (as outlined in an excellent Vulture piece earlier this year), Wright’s Gallium Studios finally ran out of funding in late 2024, leading to mass layoffs for the small team and an uncertain future for the project.

Now the idea has been revived thanks in large part to a partnership with AI middleware firm Artificial Agency. Until now, that company has mainly been focused on agentic systems that can create “virtual players” to playtest a game or serve as a partner for human players.

Now, Artificial Agency’s marketing says the company’s AI tool will be used to create “a personal behavior model and a knowledge graph” from players’ memories, drawing some unsettling comparisons to big tech surveillance.

“Meta knows what you click,” the company writes. “Netflix knows what you watch. Nobody knows what you remember. MemDNA learns from your memories locally and only with your permission.”

Wright said the partnership with Artificial Agency will let Gallium benefit from “off-the-shelf” AI solutions without having to “become an AI research company” in its own right. He also said the collaboration will help limit costs by providing a cheaper option than the “really expensive” frontier models that might be overkill for a project like this.

“Right now AI has opened a vast new design area and I’m enjoying, you know, exploring that as a designer,” Wright said.

With our unique combination of technical savvy and wide-ranging interest in the technological arts and sciences, Ars is the trusted source in a sea of information. After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

Is Apple the safe haven investors think it is? Here's a way to trade it with less risk

📅 2026-11-18  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

For months, Apple has been the undisputed haven of the technology sector. As the industry grappled with the brutal reality of Nvidia's post-Huang future, investors blindly rotated into Apple, comforting themselves with the narrative that the Cupertino giant was uniquely immune to speculative overspending on artificial intelligence.

While peers bled capital into server farms and data centers. At almost 37x forward earnings estimates, the stock is now priced for perfection, fully valued at precisely the moment the rest of the market is beginning to see multiples contract aggressively. Investors tend to huddle in the shelter of perceived safe havens, but that perceived safety can crack quickly in an adverse news cycle, as Nvidia shareholders recently learned.

One part of the news cycle finally caught up to Apple.

We've recently witnessed a bizarre parade of catastrophic system failures: the massive Microsoft outage, the FAA grounding flights, and cascading disruptions across major banking networks. Through it all, Apple was immune. Until today, when a widespread outage struck, knocking out iCloud, Apple Pay, and core support systems. The official explanation? Yet another internal update gone wrong.

It's undeniably odd how all these major tech infrastructure failures are being chalked up to routine upgrades, all clustering around the exact same narrow timeframe. Without venturing too far into the conspiratorial, the sheer coincidence of these cascading digital blackouts suggests more systemic fragility than we may have realized. That's one thing at less than 10x forward earnings, as Nvidia is, or even at a market multiple. But Apple is twice as expensive as the S&P.

Technically, Apple is balancing on a knife's edge. Shares have been relentlessly hugging the upper end of several channel-based technical indicators, such as the Bollinger Band. Apple has been priced at a steep premium for being immune to what ails other struggling stocks, but is now demonstrating similar vulnerabilities. Investors should ask themselves: what's the upside? That Apple trades at more than 40x forward earnings? 45x? Even at that rich multiple, that would represent only 18-19% upside from here. If the market suddenly decided that Apple's premium to the market would be just 50%, instead of 100%, that would be less than 28x forward earnings, a nearly 27% haircut to the current share price.

Despite the high multiple, options prices are low versus the market. Market implied volatility is well above average (around the 93rd percentile). Apple implied volatility is actually just below the 1-year average. The price of index options is in the 98th percentile relative to Apple options.

Because the stock is nominally expensive (translation? The share price is high in dollar terms), buying outright naked puts is highly capital-intensive. This is exactly where a put spread makes sense. By buying an at-the-money or slightly out-of-the-money put and simultaneously selling a lower-strike put, traders can dramatically reduce the upfront capital cost. More importantly, this structure blunts the negative impact of theta, or time decay, ensuring you don't bleed premium while waiting for the technical breakdown to materialize. Furthermore, taking advantage of the natural put skew—where further out-of-the-money puts trade at relatively higher implied volatility—lets you finance your bearish bet efficiently by selling that inflated premium.

Apple's historic premium is built on a foundation of immunity to the chaos infecting the rest of the market. Today's outage proved the company may be more vulnerable than investors appreciate.

(Programming note: I will be live on cnbc.com at 2:30 talking about all of this. Be sure to tune in.)

Haughey has pledged to buy £2 million worth of shares in the first instance if 10,000 supporters sign up. The principal ambition is to install a fans’ representative on the club’s board.

According to McStay, almost 5,000 of Celtic’s 54,000 season ticket holders have joined to date amid continued civil unrest at the club.

McStay, who plans to attend Saturday’s home game against St Mirren, is a link between the board and fans and witnessed the discontent first hand when he was invited to Parkhead last season to unfurl the title flag.

He cites the spirit of the team that overcame Hearts on the final day of the league season in 1986, when he was in his pomp in midfield, as the inspiration behind the Alliance’s efforts to reach a long-term goal.

With the Alliance, our objective is 10,000 season ticket holders signed up and Willie Haughey says he will put money in and buy shares.

He revealed he has discussed these aims with under-fire chief executive Michael Nicholson at a recent match.

“I go to games,” he said. “Last year I was back and invited as a guest to raise the flag so I was here when all the protests and boycotts were happening. The Celtic supporters want the best for their club but it is slightly different to what I think should be done. Again, that is about opinions. Even a couple of weeks ago I was at a game and I bumped into Michael – I am quite happy to give my opinions, whether that is in the dressing room with fellow players at the time or as captain talking to Fergus (McCann) or Billy McNeill or Liam Brady.”

An agm is scheduled for the start of next month. Last year’s meeting had to be cut short after Ross Desmond, Dermot’s son, was harangued while reading a pre-prepared statement from his father, who is not expected to attend this year's meeting either.

“You ask about Dermot and his position there,” said McStay. “He has put shares in the club. But to me I don’t think he is going to go anywhere soon. To me, this is a long-term objective. It might take time but we have to focus on what we are doing and build up support within our organisation to build up equity to give Celtic supporters a voice on the board. I think that will help things.”
